- **🆕🐛 [MED — SILENT NO-OP, and now a RATIFIED reject; measured 2026-07-26] `!` in an OPERAND position is accepted and completely INERT.** `s + "b"` and `!s + "b"` are **byte-identical — 0 clones both ways** (`--clones=stats`), and `int y = !a + 1` builds and prints `11`. So the user can write a consuming sigil where it means nothing, and nothing tells them. **Same silent-no-op class as the comprehension `&`.** ⚖ **Owner-ratified 2026-07-26: `!` joins `&` in rejecting at operand positions** — an operator only READS its operands, so neither sigil has anyone to grant anything to. **This is a NEW accept/reject change beyond D32's original scope: it rides D32's gauntlet, needs its own lane census (Rust · ggdef · self-host) and its own NEG fixtures.** ⚠ **The asymmetry at RESTING positions stays and is principled, not a carve-out:** `String w = !v` is **legal** (a move yields an OWNED value that can rest there) while `String w = &v` **rejects** (a borrow yields an ALIAS needing a lifetime, which Gorget deliberately lacks — the ratified no-stored-borrows rule). One rule, two kinds of result.

Production holds it for the whole enclosing scope, so a read after the closure is dead **over-rejects a sound program**. A ledger entry asserting something is filed is not evidence that it is.

### ⚖ D32 RATIFIED 2026-07-26 — `&` IS A BOUNDARY MODIFIER; `Expr::MutableBorrow` LEAVES THE AST
**Ledger: `docs/define-gorget/decisions.md`, end of LOG. Read it before scoping.** One-line rule: `&` is legal ONLY at a call argument, a for-loop iterable, a parameter declaration, and the `&self` receiver — **rejected everywhere else**; `int y = &c.fd + 1` becomes a parse error.

**The structural fact that makes this a DELETION, not a check** (measured, `gg parse`): `parse_call_arg` (`src/parser/expr.rs:2013`) calls `parse_ownership_modifier` (`src/parser/mod.rs:236`) FIRST, which **consumes** the `&` into the typed field `CallArg.ownership` — **no `Expr::MutableBorrow` node is produced at a legal boundary**. The for-loop iterable is the same (`for_loops.rs:116`). Everywhere else `&` falls through to the general prefix operator (`expr.rs:612`). **So `Expr::MutableBorrow`'s domain is EXACTLY the positions where `&` has no meaning.** Verified on one file: `bump(&c.fd)` → `ownership: MutableBorrow`, no node; `int y = 1 + &c.fd` → `node: MutableBorrow`.

**Retires (all filed this round, repros committed):** the operand-position family · the owning-position family (7 costumes) · the tainted-`Drop` duplication at non-`CallArg` positions (making `reject_tainted_formation_arg`'s two-site wiring sufficient BY CONSTRUCTION) · and it makes the **standalone `&`-formation site in `exprs/mod.rs` DEAD BY CONSTRUCTION**, dissolving Track C's "third omitted cell".
**⚠ Does NOT fix** the by-value write-through defect at the LEGAL position (`f(&c.fd)` dropping the write, 2 OK / 18 WRONG) — that stays Track C's, merely shrunk to one formation site. Track B2 is orthogonal.
**⚠ Normalization wrinkle:** parenthesized `&(a.enumerate())` at a legal boundary produces BOTH field and node — hence the four-arm match at `stmts/for_loops.rs:156-160`. Normalize into the ownership field at parse time; that match collapses to one.
**Scope:** accept/reject surface ⇒ full gauntlet, three lanes, NEG fixture per rejected position, doc write-through (`language-reference.md` §9.3, `language-design.md`). Own scout → brief → ≥3 reviews → executor.
- **⚖ D33 RATIFIED 2026-07-26 — the for-loop sigil STAYS ON THE ITERABLE (`for a in &b`).** Raised and CLOSED same day; ledger has the full reasoning. One-line why: **it already IS always a borrow** — `for x in v` is an *immutable* borrow, and `&` merely selects among three modes (bare / `&` / `!`), the same vocabulary as every other ownership boundary. The **`!` sibling is decisive**: `for item in !collection` consumes the COLLECTION, so binding-side `&` would split one axis across two syntactic positions. ⚠ `for &x in v` / `for &x in &v` are **parse errors today** — the alternatives were grammar ADDITIONS. ⚠ **Two things this ruling does NOT settle, both filed in the ledger entry:** (1) the **adapter ambiguity** (`&a.enumerate()` — what does `&` bind to? the four-arm strip at `stmts/for_loops.rs:156-160`), with a **falsifiable test**: after D32 normalizes the parenthesized `&`, that match should COLLAPSE TO ONE — if it does not, re-open placement on that evidence; (2) `for x in &v` on a **SCALAR element loses the write** (measured `1`, want `101`) — the Family-1 projected-TYPE bug, not a syntax question.

## Operating invariants (load-bearing — process/reference context, not filed work)

**NORTH STAR = RUNTIME PARITY: self-host-compiled binary produces SAME output as Rust gg.** RE-MEASURE (never trust a dated number — `*_comparison`/`runtime_diff` are diagnostic-always-pass). **Round-close procedure (the three gotchas):**
```
# 1. FORCE-REBUILD the cached self-host driver (THE load-bearing step)
rm tests/fixtures/self_host_lowerer/driver{,.c}
# 2. Regen — GG_BUILD_TIMEOUT_SECS=600 only; leave GG_TEST_TIMEOUT_SECS at default
#    (setting test timeout to 600 changes zero counts but stalls hang-class fixtures ~20×)
GG_RUNTIME_DIFF=1 GG_BUILD_TIMEOUT_SECS=600 \
  cargo test --test integration --release self_host_runtime_diff -- --nocapture
```
Read the printed `PARITY = MATCH/(...)` line and the adjudication split (ADJ-MATCH · UNADJ · BOTH-WRONG). Diagnostic-always-pass — only the printed count means anything. Floors `RUNTIME_DIFF_MATCH_FLOOR` + `GGDEF_ADJUDICATED_FLOOR` ratchet **up only**; if MATCH/ADJ rose, reseed the const in `tests/integration.rs` same commit (never lower).

**OPERATING INVARIANTS (load-bearing):**

If an environment change reintroduces gorget-1, re-verify before assuming either way.

A measured-green tight prototype may collapse to output-review→integrate.
- **Gate battery** (re-run, don't trust): `cargo test --lib` (~1085/0 debug; the 2 former `--release` `should_panic`-over-`debug_assert` reds in `src/lir/validate.rs` are now `#[cfg_attr(not(debug_assertions), ignore)]`d — fixed) + `--test lints` + `self_host_bootstrap_fixed_point` (`GG_BUILD_TIMEOUT_SECS=600` — load-bearing canary, double-frees on wrong drop model) + full integration + `self_host_runtime` (snapshot lock-in) + `*_comparison`/`runtime_diff` diagnostics. **⛳ OWNER-REQUIRED (2026-06-20): run the FULL `cargo test --test integration` at every round's close to confirm green — not just the targeted/self-host gates** (`GG_BUILD_TIMEOUT_SECS=600 cargo test --test integration -- --test-threads=4 2>&1 | tee /tmp/integration-$RANDOM.log`; debug = the clean baseline). **⛳ OWNER-REQUIRED (2026-07-23 post-mortem): the local battery must MATCH CI's test-job target set — ALSO run `cargo test -p ggdef` + `--test spec_conformance` + `--test security` (three SEPARATE targets `--test integration` never touches; three CI-red causes — ggdef gate-drift, `.filter().map()` byte-trunc MISCOMPILE, LLVM-missing conformance build-fail — hid here red for a week while rounds closed on integration-green). ## CoW / ownership / materialization

### High

- **🆕🐛💥 [HIGH — Core #8 SILENT WRONG OUTPUT, BOTH BACKENDS, an OWNERSHIP defect; found 2026-07-25 by Track-B2 review pass 4, parent-reproduced] `.map()` DESTROYS its receiver's payload for BARE-PARAM and FIELD-ACCESS receivers.** The receiver is a BORROW at those positions; the adapter consumes it anyway, so the value survives by the type system's reckoning while its heap payload is emptied underneath. Measured C and LLVM **byte-identical**: bare-param `p.map(…)` then read `p` → `10` then **`0`** (want 10/10); field-access `h.slot.map(…)` → `11` then **`0`** (want 11/11); a control program without the `.map()` prints `10/10` and `11/11`. ⚠ **A plain-LOCAL receiver is CORRECT** (`39/13`) — which is exactly why this survived: the working shape is the one existing fixtures used (Core #12 axis-blindness, receiver-shape axis). ⚠ **ggdef CANNOT adjudicate** — `.map()` is outside the phase-0 subset — so both-backends-agree is necessary-not-sufficient here; the subset gap is its own entry below. **Mechanism (`src/ir/lowering/exprs/methods.rs`, the combinator receiver-clone path ~`:3334-3366`):** the clone is gated on `p.projections.is_empty()`, so a field-access PLACE is not recognised as a place and falls to the `else` branch commented *"Non-place operand (constants, etc.) — Copy is safe"* — **false for a field place, and a Core #14 comment with no enforcing guard** (delete or guard it as part of the fix). The bare-param row takes the adjacent *"no clone fn registered — fall back to plain Copy"* branch, whose comment already concedes *"validator may flag"*. **DURABLE REPRO:** `known_gaps/sound_option_map_receiver_emptied.gg` + the `#[ignore]`d `sound_option_map_receiver_emptied` (all three receiver shapes in one fixture, the working local row wired as a live control), RED-verified at HEAD. Fix at the write site: the combinator receiver must BORROW, not consume, and the place test must recognise projections. ⚠⚠ **THE SIBLING CENSUS IS COMPLETE AND THE CLASS IS FAR WIDER THAN THIS ENTRY (Core #4; measured 2026-07-25 over the combinator set routed by `methods.rs:1631-1633`). With a RESOURCE payload (`Option[Money]`, `Money` wrapping a heap `Vector[int]`), SIX OF SEVEN combinators are broken, in THREE distinct failure modes — and `gg check` reports "OK: no semantic errors" for EVERY one:**

| combinator | `gg check` | outcome with a resource payload |
|---|---|---|
| `.map()` | OK | runs, prints **0** (want 11) — silent wrong output, **receiver-shape dependent** (plain local is CORRECT) |
| `.filter()` | OK | runs, prints **0** — same mode |
| `.or_else()` | OK | runs, prints **0** — same mode |
| `.and_then()` | OK | **SIGSEGV** on C; **LLVM refuses to compile its own IR** — type confusion, fails for a plain LOCAL too (own entry below) |
| `.flat_map()` | OK | **SIGSEGV** / LLVM won't build — same |
| `.unwrap_or_else()` | OK | **COMPILER PANIC** — `Tier 2a consume-site violation … AssignIntoOwnedSlot(dst: Money) — untracked source consumed (ownership not decided)` at `src/ir/lowering/mod.rs:2127`, i.e. `gg check` passes and then `gg build` ICEs |
| `.map_err()` | — | Result-only; not probed on `Option` — **still owed** |

⚠ **An `int` payload works for all of them** — so every trivial-payload fixture over this family is a FALSE NEGATIVE, and the corpus is full of them. This is one root (the combinator lowering does not decide ownership for a resource payload) wearing three costumes; fix it as a CLASS at the write site, not per combinator (Core #4). ⚠ The `unwrap_or_else` row is the system working as designed in one sense — the unconditional consume-site validator CAUGHT the ownership hole — but shipping it as a PANIC rather than a diagnostic is its own defect.
- **🆕🐛💥 [HIGH — TYPE CONFUSION, MEMORY-UNSAFE on C, LLVM WON'T BUILD, `gg check` ACCEPTS; measured 2026-07-25 while enumerating the `.map()` siblings] `Option[<resource>].and_then` / `.flat_map` mixes an INTEGER and a POINTER at a phi.** `gg check` → *"OK: no semantic errors"*; **C** builds and the binary **exits 139 (SIGSEGV)**; **LLVM refuses to compile its own IR** — `llc: '%v28' defined with type 'i64' but expected 'ptr'` at `%v60 = phi ptr [ %v28, %bb1 ], [ %v30, %bb2 ]`. The two incoming phi edges disagree on integer-vs-pointer; **LLVM's verifier catches what the C backend emits silently**, so the LLVM build failure is the SAME defect seen by a stricter instrument (Core #13), not a separate lane bug. ⚠ **RESOURCE-PAYLOAD-SPECIFIC** — the identical program over an `int` payload prints 42 and exits 0, so a trivial-payload fixture is a **false negative** for this class. ⚠ **NOT the `.map()` receiver-shape defect**: this fails for a **plain LOCAL** receiver too and is memory-unsafe rather than merely wrong. ⚠ ggdef **cannot adjudicate** (`.and_then()` out of phase-0 subset). **DURABLE REPRO:** `known_gaps/sound_option_and_then_resource_typeconfusion.gg` + the `#[ignore]`d `sound_option_and_then_resource_typeconfusion` (both combinators, heap-forced payload), RED-verified at HEAD. Own scout→gauntlet; fix at the write site where the combinator result slot's type is chosen; `or_else`/`map_err`/`unwrap_or_else` still owe the same probe.
- **🆕🐛 [HIGH — USER-FACING: the compiler ADVISES code it then REJECTS, in 100% of cases the advice can fire; measured 2026-07-25 by Track-B2 review pass 5, parent-reproduced] The "zero-cost move" suggestion is invalid advice.** `gg build` emits *"argument `v` is last use — pass as `!v` for zero-cost move"*; taking exactly that advice yields, at the **same span**, `error[E_OwnershipMismatch]: the parameter only borrows — it does not consume the argument; **help: remove the `!`**`. ⚠ **It is TOTAL, not a corner case** — single-writer chain verified: `move_suggestions` is pushed at exactly one site (`exprs/calls.rs:1574`) gated on `fn_consumed_params`; `fn_consumed_params` is written at exactly one site (`context.rs:1196`) inside `record_param_cloned`; `record_param_cloned` early-returns unless `is_bare_param` (`context.rs:1192`). So **every** suggestion the compiler can emit names a BARE param, and under ratified **D31 full-strict** `!` at a bare-param call site is always `E_OwnershipMismatch`. There is no input for which the advice is valid. ⚠ **Fires on `gg build`, NOT `gg check`** — so any `gg check`-based assertion is a FALSE NEGATIVE (this is why the wired test uses a new build-based helper `build_gg_expect_no_warning`). ⚠ **PRE-EXISTING at HEAD, not minted by any in-flight track** — it reaches users through `record_param_cloned`'s **SECOND** caller (`stmts/mod.rs:1892`, the throws-`Ptr` leg of `lower_return`); ⚠ **note `record_param_cloned` has TWO call sites, not one** — an executor who edits only `context.rs:2386` fixes one of two producers (Core #4). **DURABLE REPRO:** `known_gaps/sound_move_suggestion_advises_rejected_code.gg` + the `#[ignore]`d `sound_move_suggestion_advises_rejected_code`, RED-verified at HEAD. Fix at the single producer: do not suggest a move for a param the callee only borrows. (The alternative — making `!` legal there — is REJECTED: D31 ratified `!` as contractual consumption.)
- **🆕🐛 [MED — `gg run` MASKS a crashing program as an ordinary failure; measured 2026-07-25] `gg run` reports exit 1 with NO output for a binary that dies on SIGSEGV.** The same program built with `gg build` and executed directly exits **139**. So a segfaulting program is indistinguishable from a build error at the `gg run` UX, and any triage that uses `gg run` alone will mis-file a memory-safety bug as a compile failure (this happened live during the Callable-costume triage). Fix = propagate the child's signal status (128+signo, or an explicit "terminated by SIGSEGV" diagnostic). Cheap; own small track. Repro: any program in the Callable segfault family, e.g. `security/sound_callable_amp_param_projection.gg` — `gg run` → exit 1 silent; `gg build` + run → 139.
- **🆕📐 [MED — ggdef SUBSET GAP, Core #9; surfaced 2026-07-25] `.map()` and the Option/Result combinator family are outside ggdef's phase-0 subset** (*"method `.map()` is outside the phase-0 subset (may need Increment B2)"*). Consequence measured live: the `.map()` receiver-emptying defect above has **NO definitional adjudication** — the only two lanes that can see it are C and LLVM, and they agree on the wrong answer, which is precisely the configuration Core #8 says is necessary-not-sufficient. Extending the subset to the combinator family is what makes that class adjudicable; rank it accordingly in the ggdef subset-expansion queue.
- **🆕⚠ [HIGH — DESIGN CONSTRAINT discovered by Track-B2 review pass 8, ASan-measured; NOT a live bug — HEAD is correct] Routing `exprs/calls.rs:371` through `ensure_owned_at_consuming_arg` MINTS A DOUBLE-FREE.** The site clones for a `MoveParamFromBorrow` arg and **does not register the clone for drops**; the helper's Case 1 **does** (`context.rs:2513`), with no compensating `MoveZero`/mark at that site (`pre_call_clone_temps`/`consuming_clone_temps` are `methods.rs`-only). Simulated (one added `register_local`) + ASan: **`attempting double-free`** on both probes; HEAD prints `4 / done` and `1 / 3 / done`. ⚠ **Only observable when the callee moves its `!` param ONWARD** — `drop_if_alive` is self-nulling, so a callee that merely DROPS its param makes the extra drop a no-op, which is why the one pre-existing fixture over this site stayed green under the same change. **THE CALLEE-DISPOSITION AXIS (callee drops · returns it · stashes it in a longer-lived container) had ZERO coverage; two LIVE CONTROLS now committed:** `security/consume_callee_moves_on_{return,stash}.gg`, green today, RED under the unsound routing. **Consequence for any future consume-family consolidation: the five consume sites are NOT homogeneous — do not mechanically route them through one chokepoint.** Either DEFER this site, or ship the compensating `MoveZero`-and-mark.
- **🆕🧹 [MED — dead code / Core #14, measured by Track-B2 review pass 8] `exprs/methods.rs:2815` (the consuming-arg fix-up clone) appears UNREACHABLE.** Instrumented: **0 fires** across ~1200 fixtures (`tests/fixtures/` + `security/` + `known_gaps/`) **and 0 across the self-host lowerer**, while the ENCLOSING block is reached **1,940 times** compiling the self-host with `needs_clone` false every time (the enclosing block was instrumented separately per Core #13 — a silent zero is not evidence). Four hand-written shapes built to hit it (`push`/`put`/`set`/`insert` with a `Ptr(resource)` value arg, and with a field-access value arg) gave 7 reaches / 0 fires. Cause: the upstream `push/put/set` routing through `ensure_owned_at_consuming_arg` has already materialized the arg before this fix-up runs. **Disposition: investigate-then-decide — find a reaching shape, or DELETE it as an unreachable backstop** (Core #14: an explanatory comment with no enforcing guard). ⚠ Not verified against dogfood apps or the other self-host driver dirs.
- **🆕🐛 [HIGH — Core #8 + Core #10 SILENT WRITE-DROP, BOTH FACES, ggdef against us; measured 2026-07-26 by Track-C review pass 11, parent-reproduced] A STRUCT FIELD UNDER A TUPLE (`t.0.fd`) loses the write — the MIRROR of the `&v[i].0` cell.** `try_resolve_field_place` and `try_resolve_tuple_field_place` (`src/ir/lowering/exprs/mod.rs`) are a **mutually-recursive PAIR whose object-arm sets must match**, and they differ by **exactly TWO** cells: the tuple resolver lacks `Expr::Index` (→ `&v[i].0`, filed) and **the field resolver lacks `Expr::TupleFieldAccess`** (→ this). Measured, `gg check` clean, C and LLVM identical: `bumpi(&t.0.fd)` → **10** (want 11); `u.0.fd = 42` → **10** (want 42); **ggdef prints 11 / 42**. ⚠ The assign face is a **silent write-drop the round's new `E_InvalidAssignTarget` gate does NOT catch** — it is a well-formed lvalue, merely an unlowered one. Controls already correct: struct-under-struct and tuple-under-tuple both `11 / 42`; only the struct-under-TUPLE crossing is missing its arm. **Fix BOTH arms together (~4 lines each) + an ARM-PARITY LINT** — fixing one is the instance-fix Core #4 forbids; precedent in-tree at `tests/lints.rs` (`container_literal_arms_count`, `compound_assign_root_materialize_arms_count`), three place resolvers to guard. **DURABLE REPRO:** `known_gaps/sound_tupstruct_field_writethrough.gg` + the `#[ignore]`d `sound_tupstruct_field_writethrough`, RED-verified.
- **🆕🐛 [MED-HIGH — `gg check` PASSES then C EMIT REFERENCES AN UNDECLARED FUNCTION; measured 2026-07-26] Binding `.enumerate()` to a local emits a call to a nonexistent runtime symbol.** `Vector[int] v = [1,2,3]; auto e = v.enumerate()` → `gg check` clean, then the C backend emits `gorget_array_enumerate(...)` and the compile dies: *"implicit declaration of function 'gorget_array_enumerate'; did you mean 'gorget_array_insert'?"*. So `.enumerate()` works **inline in a for-loop** but not when bound. ⚠ **Design-relevant, not just a gap:** the pre-binding form (`auto e = a.enumerate(); for b in &e:`) is the natural workaround for the for-loop adapter ambiguity — and it is doubly dead, because even if it compiled, `docs/internals/cow-transient-view-model.md:254` ratifies that **BINDS MATERIALIZE (no stored borrows)**, so `&e` would write through to a private copy and never reach `a`. Own small track; likely a missing runtime shim or a lowering that should reject/inline the bind.
- **🆕🐛 [MED — SILENT GARBAGE VALUE, no `&` involved; measured 2026-07-26 while censusing the CollectionKind axis] Indexing a `Set[T]` emits a RAW ADDRESS.** `Set[int] s; s.add(seed()); print(s[0])` → `gg check` **"OK: no semantic errors"**, builds, prints e.g. `187651201204896` (ASLR-dependent; the stable fact is it is a pointer). **INTENDED: a check-time REJECTION** — a Set has no positional index, so there is no defensible element to return. Related lowering fact: `try_resolve_index_element_ptr` admits only `Array`/`OrderedMap`, so `Set`/`OrderedSet`/`Map` sit outside every place-resolution path. **DURABLE REPRO:** `known_gaps/set_index_returns_garbage.gg` + the `#[ignore]`d `set_index_returns_garbage_rejected` (asserts the reject), RED-verified. ⚠ Also unresolved on the same axis: **`&hm["k"]` on a HashMap value** loses the write (10 at base AND under the `&`-projection prototype) while the assign face `hm["k"] = 42` works — the same face-asymmetry shape, structurally out of that fix's reach.
- **🆕🐛💥 [HIGH — Core #8 SILENT WRONG OUTPUT + DUPLICATED USER `Drop`; a costume family with NO SUBJECT in either filed census; measured 2026-07-25 by Track-C review pass 9, parent-reproduced] `&`-of-a-projection in an OPERAND position.** Neither an OWNING position (ctor arg · enum payload · collection put · literal element · return · capture) nor a value/RESTING position — so the rules written for those two families **have no subject that covers it** (Core #15e-Q4: a case with no subject, which no widening of either rule fixes). **`gg check` is clean for every row.** Measured: **match scrutinee** `match &c.fd: case 10:` → C prints **0**, correct is 1 · **binary operand** `int y = &c.fd + 1` → C compile error *"invalid operands to binary + (have 'void *' and 'void *')"*, LLVM emits `add ptr` · **comparison** `if &c.fd > 5:` → compares the ADDRESS · **index expr** `v[&c.fd]` → `trap[T_Bounds]` on a raw address · **f-string** `f"{&c.fd}"` → prints the deref'd value (benign today, same lowering) · **closure expression body** `(): &c.fd` → garbage address. ⚠ **The tainted twin DUPLICATES the user `Drop`** — `if &p.fd > 5:` on a `Drop`-equipped struct prints `close 9` **TWICE** where the unsigilled control prints it once; `&` alone is the cause. ⚠ **OPERAND ORDER is its own axis and the other value is WORSE (measured): `&c.fd + 1` fails the C build, but `1 + &c.fd` / `x = x + &c.fd` / `x += &c.fd` all BUILD and print a raw ADDRESS — silent miscompile, LLVM llc-error either way. The benign-looking compile error is the LUCKY spelling; do not let it characterise the row.** (Declared omission in the repro: those spellings print a nondeterministic address, so no stable stdout pin exists.) Root (shared with the array-literal costume): **`reject_tainted_formation_arg` is wired at only TWO sites, `check_expr.rs:116` and `:757`, BOTH `CallArg`** — so the filed characterisation *"literal-element positions escape 2T"* **UNDERCOUNTS**: every non-`CallArg` position escapes. ⚠ **ggdef CANNOT adjudicate this family** ("expression 'unsupported' is outside the phase-0 subset" for match/binop/index/f-string) though it DOES rule on the array-literal owning position — so C/LLVM agreement is worthless here; **ggdef subset gap owed** (Core #9). **DURABLE REPROS:** `known_gaps/sound_amp_operand_position_scrutinee.gg` (asserts `1`) + `security/sound_amp_operand_position_duplicate_drop.gg` (asserts a single `close 9`), both `#[ignore]`d and RED-verified at HEAD. Likely reference-grade fix: **REJECT** `&`-of-a-place in an operand position (an operand is a read; the sigil is meaningless there) — a check-time error + negative fixtures, not a lowering change.
- **🆕🐛 [HIGH — Core #8 + Core #4, LAST face of the write-through class; measured 2026-07-25 by Track-C review pass 4] `&`-of-a-GET-CHAIN field loses the write, at base AND after the Track-C fix.** `f(&v.get(i).unwrap().field)` on `Vector[Counter]` prints **10** (want 11) on **C and LLVM**, base and prototype — while the **assign** face (`b.get(0).unwrap().fd = 11`) and the **compound-assign** face (`+= 1`) both write through correctly, and **ggdef prints 11**. Root: `try_resolve_field_place`'s object arms are Identifier / SelfExpr / FieldAccess / Deref / Index (`src/ir/lowering/exprs/mod.rs:2780-2840`) — **no method-chain arm**. The assign face does not need one because it falls back to `resolve_ptr_field_place` (`stmts/assigns.rs:838`), whose own comment documents exactly this shape (*"including a `.get().unwrap()` method chain, which lowers to a Ref per the ratified auto-borrow-from-get"*, `:784-787`). **Fix (~12 lines, MEASURED by the reviewer): give the shared producer the same fallback** — when `try_resolve_field_place` returns `None`, `lower_expr` the object and route through `resolve_ptr_field_place` (make it + `PtrFieldPlace` + `mod assigns` `pub(in crate::ir::lowering)`). One resolver, both faces, one source of truth.

Measured: `a.fd = 11` in a closure IS classified `BorrowCaptureMode::Mutable` and the outer read correctly rejects `E_ReadWhileMutCaptured`; `bumpi(&b.fd)` is **not classified**, so check passes and the write vanishes. Under **D31 full-strict `&` at a call site IS contractually a write** → a Core #4 sibling-site hole in an enumerated set whose own doc-comment (`:217-219`) states the closed list. **Fix is a few lines** and converts a silent miscompile into an existing diagnostic. ⚠ ggdef cannot adjudicate (closures are outside phase-0). Durable repros owed for both rows. ⚠ **WHY IT IS ON THE WRONG SIDE (the boundary model, ratified 2026-07-26 and written into `language-reference.md`/`language-design.md`): a boundary is where a value CROSSES FROM ONE SCOPE INTO ANOTHER, and the sigil is written AT the crossing. For a closure the crossing is at CAPTURE — and Gorget has **no capture-list syntax**, so it INFERS the mode from the body instead. An in-body sigil therefore sits AFTER the crossing has already happened, which is precisely why it can never take effect.** ⚠⚠ **AND THE TARGET SHAPE IS ALREADY RATIFIED — D7 (capture-syntax package, completing D5): per-variable capture lists `(&count)(): …` / `(!name, &total)(x): …`, bare names REJECTED in a capture list, `!():` move-all sugar, `&`-capture exclusivity LIVENESS-based (borrow ends at the closure's last use; production's scope-based behaviour is a filed conformance gap).** Measured: `(&count)():` is a **parse error at HEAD** — grammar work (two-group lookahead) sits on the D5 implementation track. **So the fix is NOT a fork:** the sigil belongs in the capture list, and the in-body sigil should REJECT once that syntax exists. Until then it is a silent no-op, which is the defect this entry files. ⚠ **SIBLING, same construct, different symptom — likely ONE track:** the closure-body `&`-of-self/param FORMATION **cc-fail** (int32 passed where `void*` expected) filed separately below. **DURABLE REPRO (committed — covers BOTH rows in one fixture): `known_gaps/sound_closure_amp_formation_inert.gg` + the `#[ignore]`d `sound_closure_amp_formation_inert`, asserting the INTENDED `11 / 11`; RED-verified C `10/10`, LLVM `10/10`, with the top-level control printing `11` to prove it is the closure body and not the shape.**

- **🆕🛡 [HIGH — the Core #6 guard shape for D10; measured 2026-07-25] A participant-construction guard CANNOT see UNROUTED sites — it must be an exhaustive NO-WILDCARD match over `Expr` variants.** A private-`PlaceParticipant`-constructor + exhaustive-`match` limb only fires where participants are already built; a site that never routes constructs nothing, which is the exact failure mode it was meant to fix — and the comprehension and match-guard costumes above are today's proof the class stays open. - **🆕⚖️ [HIGH — D10 NEEDS **TWO** CLAUSES ON TWO AXES, not one; measured 2026-07-25 by the live-range scout] The "everything is a borrow until mutated or consumed" model covers only the READER-vs-WRITER axis.** Framed around *a borrow being invalidated*, it structurally misses **`f(&v, &v)` — two writers, no live borrow at all**; **four in-repo NEG fixtures went GREEN** under the live-range-only prototype. So D10 = **R0 exclusivity** (at most one writer/mover per overlapping place — writer×writer) **+ R1/R2 invalidation** (a live borrow must not be invalidated during its live range). R1/R2 *derives* D10(b) ADDENDUM 3 and its same-day amendment instead of asserting them; R0 must be kept untouched alongside. ⚠ **Any brief claiming "one rule" ships a memory-safety regression** — recorded because the single-model framing was attractive and nearly landed.

Write-through VALUES are already correct (4 then 2) — only the drop of the old value is missing. **PRE-EXISTING and independent of the return boundary** (identical before and after Track B1); it was MASKED in the combined costume `local = v; v = build(2); return local`, where the bind's double-free cancelled the missing drop — so fixing the bind correctly UNMASKED it. (`security_known_unsafe` is the wrong harness: it runs `detect_leaks=0`, so a pure leak cannot trip it.) Fix at the write-through re-assign: drop the old pointee before storing the new one. - **🆕🐛💥 [HIGH — MEMORY-UNSAFE, both backends] An `equip` method whose body is an EXPRESSION-BODY return of its own OWNING `!` param USE-AFTER-FREES.** The statement-return path move-zeroes a returned `!` param (`owning_param_returned` → `move_zero_and_mark`); the FOUR expression-body return sites in `functions.rs` do not, so the callee's exit `drop_if_alive *p` frees the buffer the returned value aliases. Fix at the class, not per-site: the four expression-body returns need the same owning-param transfer the statement return already performs (a shared helper, or hoist the move-zero to the one place both forms converge). **Burning this down is the precondition for promoting the `AssignIntoReturnSlot` consume-site class to FATAL** — it is the ONE violation across the 1676-fixture corpus (measured; regenerate with `GG_VALIDATE_CONSUME_SITES=<log>` + `gg build --emit-gir` over `tests/fixtures/*.gg`).

- **🆕🧹 [MED — Layering, typed metadata] The `&`/`!`-param auto-deref site reads the WRONG typed bit for "is this an owning param".** `exprs/mod.rs`'s `let is_move_param = ctx.is_owned_local(builder, local_id)` is documented as "`!` params (owned): use Move to transfer ownership", but a `!` param's `ownership` is `Borrowed { Param(self), Unique }` — identical to a `&` param — so `is_owned_local` is FALSE and that branch never fires for the shape its comment names. The single typed bit that distinguishes them is `is_owning_param` (`set_owning_param`'s own doc says so). Measured live: adding a `&`-param-only ownership tag to the else-branch immediately mis-tagged `!` params in the self-host (`Lexer__lex_emit`, `Parser__mk_expr/mk_type/mk_pat`, `parser___decompose_with_stmt`) and produced 10 consume-site violations until `is_owning_param` was excluded explicitly. Switching the predicate to `is_owning_param` is the write-through, but it CHANGES the emitted shape for every `!`-param read (Move + `set_owned` instead of Copy), so it needs its own measurement pass. Grep the other `is_owned_local`-as-a-proxy-for-owning-param sites at the same time.

- **🆕📐 [MED — charter] A `String &`-param bind still costs ONE clone where a hand-writer pays ZERO.** The other resource types bind a `&`-param as a zero-cost CoW Ptr alias; String cannot, because a Copy-mode store of a String read through a pointer is `gorget_string_copy_cow` at BOTH backends (an eager deep copy), not the shallow `memcpy` every other aggregate gets. The frontend now tags that temp honestly (drop-registered + `FreshOwned`, which is what removed the double-clone at the return boundary), but the eager copy itself is a backend-layer decision the GIR cannot express: `AssignMode::Copy` means "shallow alias" for `GorgetArray` and "deep clone" for `GorgetString`. Reference-grade fix: make the GIR say which it means (the LIR already spells the shallow form as `SlotStore { is_move: true }`, and `insts.rs`'s own comment calls that "a SHALLOW byte-copy (an alias / borrow)"), then let the boundary chokepoints materialize uniformly. Related: the alias-at-bind leaves the now-dead auto-deref temp emitted (a wasted 64-byte struct `memcpy` per bind — no allocation, but it should be DCE'd or not emitted).

- **🆕🛡 [MED — Core #6 burn-down] Promote `ConsumeSiteClass::AssignIntoReturnSlot` from the non-fatal `assign_warnings` partition to `fatal_warnings`.** The class exists and fires (4 unit tests in `src/ir/validate.rs` pin it firing on borrowed AND untracked sources, and staying silent on the sound owned-and-dead transfer and on the borrow-contract return). It is routed non-fatally because a new class is fatal the instant it lands and the corpus count is not yet zero. Run the burn-down with `GG_RETURN_SLOT_GUARD=fatal`; when the count reaches zero, delete the partition + the env gate and let it join the other six classes. Re-measure the count before acting on it.

- **🆕🐛💥 [HIGH — SOUNDNESS, MEMORY-UNSAFE, both backends, adversarial audit 2026-07-22, parent-reproduced+ASan-confirmed] The D10 "one exclusive writer" rule — the load-bearing invariant the whole lazy-CoW model rests on — is NOT structurally enforced.** `check_call_aliasing` (`src/semantic/safety/helpers.rs:1472`) is a SYNTACTIC place-overlap check spread across 3 call-shaped arms (`check_expr.rs:316` Call / `:438` MethodCall / `:1304` DotShorthand), with costume holes that produce heap-use-after-free. Confirmed holes (ASan `gg build --sanitize`, C backend; both backends share the semantic pass): **#1 (broadest) get-chain element VIEW as a call arg** — `.get()/.first()/.last().unwrap()` are invisible participants (no MethodCall arm in `find_root_def_id_with_path` helpers.rs:567) → `f(v.get(0).unwrap(), &v)` accepts; resource element → UAF in `gorget_string_copy_cow`. **#2 method RECEIVER not a participant** (`check_call_aliasing(args)` only; receiver walked separately) → `v.grow_from(&v)` accepts → UAF. **#3 module-level `Static` roots excluded** by the `DefKind::Variable`-only filter (helpers.rs:1494; globals are Static, resolve.rs:714) → `f(G,&G)` accepts+UAF while byte-identical LOCAL `f(L,&L)` REJECTS (clean isolated proof; parent-repro'd c10c-vs-c10d). **#4 for-loop body mutation via a `&`-arg free call** (`for x in v: grow(&v)`) opaque to the iterable guard (check_expr.rs:576) → UAF; direct `v.push` in body rejects. **#5 match-scrutinee element binding across a mutating arm** (`case Some(x)` over `v.first()`, then `v.push`) not recorded as a CoW-borrow source → UAF (for-loop has a guard, match has none). **#6 MED KNOWN-DEFERRED** `v[i] += mutate(&v)` should reject per D10(b) addendum-2 (RV-C track), doesn't. Structural half HOLDS: stored `&`-bindings (`Type &name=...`) rejected at parse (LocalBorrowBindSigil). ROOT CAUSE: check is spelled over SYNTAX not SEMANTICS (sees `v[i]` not `v.get(i)`, args not receiver, Variable not Static) + scattered across 3 arms, not one chokepoint. **⚖ RULED (owner 2026-07-22, "reference-grade, no question"): (b) STRUCTURAL.** Rebuild as ONE semantic place-overlap chokepoint that EVERY call routes through (free-fn / method incl. RECEIVER / operator-overload / index-store `__set`/`__put` / closure / constructor), with a complete participant model + a SEMANTIC place-resolver that descends `.get()/.first()/.last().unwrap()` view-chains (reuse `find_get_chain_taint_root` helpers.rs:512 / `find_collection_source_with_path` :602), the receiver (implicit sigil: `&self`⇒writer, bare `self`⇒reader), and `Static` roots — Core #4 centralize-at-producer, retire the 3 syntactic arms. Land WITH a Core #6 guard forcing every future call-lowering shape through the single check (arm-count / unavoidable-by-construction) so costume #7 can't reopen it. Patching-the-syntactic-check is REJECTED. Also fold the deferred #6 (`v[i] += mutate(&v)`, D10(b) addendum-2 / RV-C) into the same chokepoint. Own scout→brief→≥3 reviews→gauntlet ON OWNER GO; the scout designs the chokepoint grounded in the D10 ledger + layering-discipline doc (devbook/24); neg fixture per hole; ASan-gate; both lanes; the audit's for/match separate nets (iterable guard, match) get subsumed or kept-and-covered. Own scout→brief→gauntlet after the ruling; neg fixture per hole (get/first/last/dict × arg-order; receiver; Static; for; match; String elements); ASan-gate; both lanes. **DURABLE REPROS (committed 2026-07-25, one per costume, all RED-verified at HEAD — each passes `gg check` then ASan-aborts with a heap-use-after-free; all `#[ignore]`d asserting the INTENDED `E_BorrowConflict` reject, in `tests/fixtures/security/` + `security.rs`):** `sound_excl_getchain_local` (#1) · `sound_excl_getchain_static` (#3) · `sound_excl_getchain_fieldpath` (#1 field-path) · `sound_excl_getchain_alias_provenance` (bind alias) · `sound_excl_receiver_writer` (#2) · `sound_excl_forbody_amp_writer` (#4 — ⚠ needs ≥2 elements; the 1-element shape is a FALSE NEGATIVE) · `sound_excl_nested_writer_{call,closure,index_store,ctor,tuple}` (writer nested ONE CALL DEEP, per call shape) · `sound_excl_overload_alias_twin` (⚠ built on `Acc b = a; a + b`; `a + a` runs CLEAN and is false confidence). Hole #5 has the OPPOSITE polarity — see the match-scrutinee MATERIALIZE entry below. LIVE over-rejection guards that must stay green: `sound_ctor_mover_sibling_accept` (ADDENDUM 3's mover exemption), `sound_ctor_mover_first_reject` (must stay `E_UseAfterMove`, the LIVENESS axis), `sound_getchain_sever_accept` (intra-function sever). ⚠ **ggdef is NOT the oracle for this class** — measured 2026-07-25, it ACCEPTS every in-subset fixture above: it is a value-semantics interpreter, so realloc-invalidation is structurally unobservable there. ggdef owes the same reject as part of this landing (Core #9). Session-scratch probes /tmp/excl_probes/ superseded by the committed fixtures.

- **🐛💥 [HIGH — MEMORY-SAFETY, both backends, R1 — box-fix output-review 2026-07-24, parent-reproduced] Moving a `Box[T]` via `!bx` into a consuming `!`-param fn BAD-FREES a stack address.** `void consume(Box[int] !b): print("consumed")` + `Box[int] bx = Box(42); consume(!bx)` is ACCEPTED by `gg check` but ASan-aborts `attempting free on address which was not malloc()-ed` (C) / SIGABRT (LLVM) on ALL `Box[T]` (int/String/struct, verified). The `!`-param Box CONSUMPTION ABI passes the box param as `&slot` (the caller's stack address) and the callee `free`s it → freeing a stack address (miscompile of an accepted program). Pre-existing + ORTHOGONAL to the 2026-07-24 scope-exit-drop fix (that touches the LOCAL's drop, not the `!`-param ABI). More severe than the two box siblings below (a memory-safety abort, not a leak/compile-break). **known_gaps repro: `tests/fixtures/security/box_move_param_bad_free.gg`** (`#[ignore]`d `security_safe` test `box_move_param_bad_free_safe`; expected `consumed\ndone`). - **🐛 [HIGH — pre-existing C-emit name collision, BOTH backends, found 2026-07-24 fixing the Box[resource-struct] leak] `Box__<inner>__drop` is DEFINED TWICE for `Vector[Box[R]]` and nested `Box[Box[R]]` when R needs a recursive drop.** `helpers.rs:313` emits `static inline void Box__R__drop(Box__R self){GORGET_FREE(...)}` (the box's trivial free-helper) while `emit_box_drop_wrappers` (`emit_types.rs:1623`) emits the recursive `void Box__R__drop(void* slot)` wrapper — both are referenced the moment a Box is a collection ELEMENT or a nested-box payload → C `redefinition of 'Box__R__drop'` / LLVM runtime-compile failure. This is a COMPILE BREAK, not a leak (the program never builds). `Option[Box[R]]` and struct-field `Option[Box[R]]` are FINE (build + LSan-clean — those paths don't emit the free-helper). This collision is ALSO what blocks fixing the nested-`Box[Box[R]]` LEAK: routing the outer box's drop through the inner `Box__R__drop` wrapper (the natural fix) trips exactly this redefinition. Fix = one owner of the `Box__R__drop` symbol — give the trivial free-helper a distinct name (mirror the `__gorget_box_free_R` scheme) OR suppress it when the recursive wrapper is emitted. Own scout→gauntlet; ASan + build-both-backends gate; add `Vector[Box[resource-struct]]` + `Box[Box[resource-struct]]` fixtures that must BUILD and be LSan-clean. **known_gaps repro: `tests/fixtures/known_gaps/box_element_drop_symbol_collision.gg`** (`#[ignore]`d integration test `box_element_drop_symbol_collision`, expected `done`). ⚠ CORRECTION (verified 2026-07-24): only the collection-ELEMENT case (`Vector[Box[R]]`) compile-breaks; nested `Box[Box[R]]` actually BUILDS (it LEAKS instead — file that separately, do not lump).

The full drop lives in the UNIFIED `__gorget_dtor_R` (`user fn + field frees`, `type_drop_fns[R].drop_fn_name` for user-drop/collision types). Repro: `struct Money{String label}; equip Money with Drop: void drop(!self): print("x")`; `Box[Money] bx = Box(Money(heaped()))` → LSan 22-byte leak (the user `drop` DOES run — "x" prints — but `label` is never freed). Fix the CLASS at both sites: target `type_drop_fns[R].drop_fn_name` (= `__gorget_dtor_R` for user-drop/collision, `R__drop` for plain Recursive) — the same unified full-drop a plain `Money` local uses. Touches `box_inner_drop_fn` (WIDE — the wrapper is shared by Vector/Option/field Box contexts) → own scout→gauntlet, ASan-gate every wrapper context. Distinct from the Recursive-struct/enum leak fixed 2026-07-24 (that was a MISSING match arm; this is a WRONG-fn-selection shared with the wrapper). **known_gaps repro: `tests/fixtures/security/box_user_drop_struct_field_leak.gg`** (`#[ignore]`d `security_safe_no_leak` test `box_user_drop_struct_field_no_leak`, expected `done\ndropping`; verified 2026-07-24 — user `drop` runs, `label` leaks 22B).

- **🔬 SH-COW SCOUT CORRECTION (2026-07-21, decision-ready — SUPERSEDES the inverted premises in the block below):** (1) The "class-A #3 ggdef lie" is **STALE** — ggdef now write-throughs `.get()`-chain field-stores correctly (get-chain-as-place, `spec/ggdef/src/ggc.rs:338`/`eval.rs:812`, landed 2026-07-19). **NO ggdef work in this campaign.** (2) The bare-param claim is **INVERTED**: ggdef (the truth axis) says a BARE-param `.get()`-chain mutation MATERIALIZES (caller unchanged) — so the SH's `insts=0`/`20` is **CORRECT** and **Rust gg's `insts=2`/`21` is the DEFECT** (Rust fails to materialize a bare-param root AND omits the dead-write warning it correctly emits for the `.push`/`[i]=`/`.n=` siblings). This is the ratified CoW model (bare = immutable borrow → materialize; `&` = write-through) — a "reference lags self-host" SUCCESSION data point; Rust oracle-hygiene bug filed separately below. (3) The GENUINE SH gap = **Face A on OWNED-LOCAL and `&`-PARAM roots** (`.get(i).unwrap().field = v` / `OP= v`): ggdef+Rust AGREE on write-through (owned `15,13,99`; `&`-param `21,22,66`), SH DROPS it (`10,10,10` / `20,20,20`). Needs its OWN scout+prototype — has an unresolved DESIGN tension, do NOT band-aid.] The Track-1 fix made refcount handles (Shared/Weak/Channel) clone-if-live at CONSUMING positions (ctor/literal/push/put — landed, both consuming clones verified ASan-clean). The PARAM/boundary side is untouched and has a sigil-aware design gap:** `needs_param_drop` (`src/ir/types.rs:573`) keys on TYPE only (`Trivial && drop_strategy!=None && clone_fn.is_some()`) and CANNOT distinguish bare `Shared s` (borrow — register_param must NOT drop; the return-boundary must incref) from `Shared !s` (owned — must drop). Picking either side without a SIGIL-AWARE redesign yields a refcount double-count OR the plain-call UAF. **THREE repros (all MEASURED @HEAD 596a3e16 = PRE-EXISTING, my Track-1 change did NOT alter them):** (a) **plain-call param UAF** — `void takes(Shared[int] x): print(x.strong_count())` then `takes(s); s.strong_count()` → heap-use-after-free in `gorget_shared_strong_count` (the bare param is a borrow but `needs_param_drop(Shared)=true` so `takes` decrefs it at scope exit → `s` freed under the caller). (b) **passthrough-return** — `Shared[int] passthrough(Shared[int] s): return s`, caller keeps `s` → UAF in `gorget_shared_drop` (the return boundary — `ensure_owned_at_boundary` Case 2, `context.rs`, UNWIDENED for refcount — does not incref). (c) **Shared-captured-by-closure** — a `Callable` capturing a live Shared routes EXCLUSIVELY through `ensure_owned_at_boundary` (`closures.rs`), same boundary fn; its baseline was ASan-clean in the Track-1 matrix but is the same family. Plus the **`!Shared` param** enumeration: `is_mut_ref_param`/`is_ref_param` are both false for a non-resource Move type, so a `!Shared` param reaches NEITHER param path today — the model map must cover it. **needs_param_drop UNIFICATION note:** it is the sibling of the Track-1 consuming accessor `is_refcount_clone_type` but is NOT interchangeable (the third `drop_strategy!=None` clause excludes Channel, which is `DropStrategy::None`); the sigil redesign is where the two converge. Fix = a sigil-aware param ownership model (borrow vs owned `!`) driving register_param drop + the return/capture boundary incref, coherent across all three families. Own scout→brief→gauntlet; Track 1 deliberately did NOT touch `needs_param_drop` or `ensure_owned_at_boundary`.

- **🆕⚖️ [MED — OWNER-RULED during the paused CoW-aliasing round (between 2026-07-22 and 07-24; exact date not recovered from the session record — do not re-date it precisely without evidence). NOT YET IMPLEMENTED] Match-scrutinee element binding across a mutating arm = MATERIALIZE, not reject.** `case Some(x)` over `v.first()` followed by a mutation of `v` in the arm (hole #5 of the D10-exclusivity entry above). **Ruling: this is the TEMPORAL axis** (`CowBorrowMutation` materialize — the same axis as the intra-function CoW sever, where the mutation site is VISIBLE so the clone can be placed lazily), **NOT the call-arg D10(b) place-overlap reject axis** (where the mutation hides behind a call boundary and the only rescue would be a speculative unconditional clone). Consistent with the ratified single-element materialize+warn. Implement the match-binding materialize; keep it OUT of the place-overlap chokepoint. Both lanes + a fixture asserting the pre-mutation value. **DURABLE REPRO:** `tests/fixtures/security/sound_match_scrutinee_elem_materialize.gg` + `security.rs::sound_match_scrutinee_elem_materialize_safe` (`#[ignore]`d, asserts the INTENDED pre-mutation value; RED-verified at HEAD — `gg check` clean then ASan heap-use-after-free in `gorget_string_copy_cow`). ⚠ Its `#[ignore]` reason says so explicitly: **a fix that REJECTS this program has implemented the wrong ruling.** Out of ggdef's subset (`.first()` is Increment B2). **Un-ignore when the match-binding materialize lands.**

- **🆕 ROUND-8 FOLLOW-UP (filed 2026-06-28, from the get_or ownership executor `ac9980be`):**
  - **🐛 TEMPORARY resource default to `Dict.get_or`/`get_or_put` leaks on a HIT (pre-existing, CoW move-elision vs borrow-wrapper interaction).** `dh.get_or("k", Holder(x))` — passing a TEMP resource default (not a named local) — leaks on the HIT path (the existing value is returned, the temp default is unused). Confirmed PRE-EXISTING on the baseline driver (String temp default, 9-byte leak), INDEPENDENT of the `e81233ac` clone fix (which clones for the borrowing wrapper). Root: the move-eligible temp is marked moved-into-the-call (CoW last-use), but the get_or wrapper takes the default BY BORROW + only clones it on miss → on a hit the temp is never consumed/dropped. The `e81233ac` fixtures use NAMED-LOCAL defaults (the brief's shape), so this is unexercised by them. Fix needs the call-site move/borrow decision reconciled with the borrowing-wrapper ABI (drop the temp at the call, or pass-by-value-and-let-wrapper-drop). Check both compilers. Own scout.

- **🆕 ROUND-7 FOLLOW-UPS (filed 2026-06-28, from the Option-combinator scout `a96dc0e2` + the Dict.filter executor `a3c3d622`):**
  - **⭐ ROUND-8 LEAD — resource-valued `Dict.get_or`/`get_or_put` ownership double-free in BOTH compilers (Core #8 reference defect; DEEP; scouted `a0728051`).** Int-valued `get_or_put` LANDED `dfdfa174`→`3f8f7883` (+2, trivials correct + ASan-clean, see DONE). The C1 review `af078272` flagged it half-implemented + a shared ownership bug; the completion scout `a0728051` REPRODUCED + CORRECTED the diagnosis end-to-end (sanitizers):
    - **R1 (one line, COUPLED — do NOT ship alone):** `lower_types.gg:2378` `result_type_for_method` `if method == "get_or":` not extended to `get_or_put` → result slot falls to `I64_TYPE` → resource-valued `Dict[String,String].get_or_put` CC-FAILs `int64_t = Str` (Rust accepts). Fix = `"get_or" or "get_or_put"`. But R1 alone turns the CC-FAIL into an executing double-free (R2).
    - **R2 — the REAL write-site (Core #1) + the WIDER Core #8 bug:** NOT `gorget_map_put` move-vs-clone (the C1 review's "Rust leaks" claim is STALE — Rust is CLEAN for String: `copy_cow` into the result slot + a separate map clone, `src/backend/c_lir/mod.rs:2318`). The self-host bug = the wrapper RETURNS the caller's BORROWED `__d` (`lir_codegen.gg:915` get_or_put / `:919` get_or); the self-host passes the default by BORROW (fn-call default-borrow, no consuming clone unlike Rust's inline LIR) → frees both the original default AND the returned result → DOUBLE-FREE (ASan-confirmed for String, get_or + get_or_put). **The WIDER Core #8 finding:** for NON-String resource values (`Dict[String, Vector[int]]`) **Rust gg ITSELF double-frees on BOTH C AND LLVM** (its special-case only covers `val_is_str` `insts.rs:3171`; a non-String aggregate gets a shallow memcpy, not a deep clone) — AND so does self-host. Latent (no corpus fixture exercises the owned-live-default resource shape under ASan), but the textbook "both backends agree on the wrong answer."
    - **Reference-grade fix (DEEP, both-compiler):** clone EACH owned output of the wrapper, leaving the borrowed `__d` to the caller, using a TYPED resource-clone (not String-only). Self-host: `dict_get_or_wrapper_for` (`lir_codegen.gg:915/919`) via the existing `lc_collection_clone_fn` (`lir_codegen.gg:1653`, resolves any value type → `*_clone_inplace`), gated on resource-ness (trivials keep move-and-return-same → int fixture stays green). Rust: `try_emit_dict_get_or` (`src/lir/lower/insts.rs:3037`) deep-clone for ANY resource value, typed-metadata-driven (shared LIR → C+LLVM). Covers get_or + get_or_put. Prototype proved the String wrapper-clone shape ASan/Leak-clean; resource-generic is the remaining work. **+ a language-design call (Core #8):** reject resource-valued get_or/get_or_put that can't be cheaply cloned, OR define the clone semantics — ground in `docs/devbook/11-copy-on-write.md` §"Materialization points" + `docs/devbook/13-ownership-in-ir.md`. Deliverables: R1 one-liner + self-host typed clone + Rust resource clone + ASan/Leak fixtures for `Dict[String,String]` AND `Dict[String,Vector[int]]` × {get_or, get_or_put} on C+LLVM+self-host bootstrap; int `dict_get_or_put` stays green. **Own brief→≥3 reviews→executor (touches Rust src + a language-design decision).**
  - **🐛 `coroutine_option_result` WRONG-OUTPUT on CLEAN baseline (pre-existing async/task-ordering bug).** Surfaced by the get_or_put/bare-None scout `a68c3742`: async task results (lines 26-27) expect `1,2` but get `0,0`. Reproduces on the pristine baseline (NOT a regression from any round-7 fix), unrelated to Option/Dict combinators. Likely the same async frame-field / task-result-ordering class as `coroutine_dict_set`. Own scout with the async-coroutine cluster.
  - **🐛 `coroutine_dict_set` WRONG-OUTPUT — async frame-field promotion gap (filter-independent, DEEPER).** A coroutine that `fold`s + `return`s a result (then `await`ed) yields `0`. Reproduces with a minimal `fold`+`return`+`await` coroutine and NO filter → it's an async frame-field promotion gap (the fixture's own comment warns: "must use frame-field access f->_N in the poll function"), not a HOF bug. Own scout when the async-coroutine cluster is attacked.
  - **🐛 bare `Ok(..)`/`Error(..)` unconstrained combinator chains = Core #8 BOTH-COMPILER miscompile + a LANGUAGE-DESIGN fork (MIRAGE for parity; PHASE-2-aligned). SCOUTED `a32e021d` 2026-06-28.** A truly-bare `Ok(5).map(c).unwrap_or(0)` with NO sibling `Result[T,E]` in scope MISCOMPILES IN BOTH compilers: Rust gg link-fails (C) / returns garbage (throws) / prints wrong (interpreter), `gg check` ACCEPTS it; self-host CC-FAILs (`GorgetArray = int`, the Some-twin). Rust only compiles it correctly when a sibling Result constrains the unbound `E` via its UNIFICATION ENGINE (`typecheck.rs:4472-4490` fresh_type_var; `ir/lowering/exprs/mod.rs:1533/1588` `resolve_option_result_variant` returns None when neither expected_type nor throws-result is a Result → falls through to a plain i64 `@Ok` = the miscompile). **MIRAGE for parity (+0):** only `test_result_chaining.gg` is blocked corpus-wide, and it sits BEHIND a 2nd independent blocker (the Result-producing closure-return-type bug, separately filed below / `result_map`). Everything else in the Result cluster already MATCHES (typed `Result[T,E]` locals supply `expected_type`). **The real finding = a language-design decision (own scout→brief targeting the RUST TYPECHECK FIRST, then mirror self-host):** what should fully-unconstrained `Ok(5).map(...)` DO — (a) REJECT with `gg check` "cannot infer error type for `Ok`; annotate the Result" (the Rust-grade, Core-#8-correct outcome + negative fixture), (b) default the unobserved type param consistently in BOTH typecheck+lowerers (a silent guess — diverges if E later unifies to a resource), or (c) status quo (silent-miscompile/CC-FAIL — NOT acceptable). The self-host arm CANNOT be designed in isolation (mirror whatever Rust is fixed to do); do NOT ship a self-host default-E guess Rust doesn't make ("redesign around a compiler gap" in reverse). Enforcement-flavored (needs the unbound-E unification verdict, NOT a simple FP-enumerable single-node check) → **PHASE-2-aligned (the deferred general enforcement pass) OR an explicit owner reject-vs-default call.** Self-host write site for the eventual mirror: `lower_expr.gg:6420-6431` (the `Some` arm to mirror), reads `ctx.expected_type` (carries `Result__T__E` when present) + would need `fn_is_throws` (`lower.gg:418`) widened to carry the throws Result T/E. **⭐ FOLDED INTO THE ERROR-MODEL DESIGN (owner 2026-06-28): the reject-vs-default-vs-existential decision is now `the error-model design notes (git history)` §9 Q17 — decide it when the error model is revisited, NOT as a standalone fix.** Cross-language survey DONE (in §9 Q17): camp (a) REJECT = Rust/Haskell/Swift-explicit-Result (recommended, fits Gorget's monomorphized `Result[T,E]`); (b) DEFAULT-to-universal-existential = Swift `any Error`/Go `error`/Zig `anyerror` (needs an existential Gorget lacks — a deliberate feature, NOT a bug fix; nobody defaults `E` to a concrete `Unit`/`Never`); (c) INFER-from-body = Zig error sets (different error model). Enforcement-flavored → PHASE-2-aligned; Rust-typecheck-first then mirror self-host.
  - **🐛 `and_then`/`or_else` closure returning bare `None()` mis-slots (blocks `test_option_chaining`, +1).** After the `Some`-chain fix lands, `Some(3).and_then((x): None())` advances to a DIFFERENT error (`int64_t = Option__int64_t`) — the closure's bare `None()` return mis-types in the `combinator_closure_ret_type` / bare-`None` typing path. Worth tackling (flips `test_option_chaining`). Scout the bare-`None`-in-combinator-closure typing; likely `lower_types.gg`/`lower_expr.gg`. Tackle after the `Some` fix.

- **🆕🐛 [MED — pre-existing, filed by the stage-1b TRACK-1 executor 2026-07-19] `Set[String]` uses the byte-based `gorget_ordered_set_new` variant, not `_new_str`** (`lower_set_literal_from_array`, `collections.rs:223` never selects the str-keyed ctor). So a String-keyed set has `key_drop=NULL` + no `key_materialize` → it cannot own a HEAP String key (leaks it) and dedups by BYTES not content. Benign only for static-literal elements (no heap, `.rodata`-aliased). This BLOCKS the Defect-A set-literal clone-if-live fix: routing a live String element through `ensure_owned_at_consuming_arg` produces a heap clone that the byte-variant set then leaks — so the set picker fix was REVERTED (the live-source `Set[String] s = {x, ..}` shape stays a pre-existing "shallow copy of resource" validator panic). Fix (own track, has set-dedup SEMANTIC implications — byte→content): detect a String/Hashable-resource element type in the set literal and select `gorget_set_new_str`/`gorget_ordered_set_new_str`, THEN re-apply the clone-if-live picker (thread the element AST expr into `insert_elem`, route through `ensure_owned_at_consuming_arg` — the diff is in `/tmp/sharedmove_exec_M5.patch`) and add a live-source `Set[String]` fixture. Tuple literals were audited and already clone-if-live correctly (no work).

- **[MED — under-materialize; CoW 2G step-4 gap, deferred by design] A user `&self`-mutator receiver in a loop is not caught by the pre-header prescan.** The prescan is an UNTYPED AST walk; it can't resolve that a user method NAME (`b.compact()`, first param MutPtr) mutates its receiver, so the loop-carried materialize doesn't fire → per-iteration throwaway (the in-body materialize DOES fire via the typed `fn_sigs` lookup in `needs_mut`, `exprs/methods.rs:~1846`, but is reverted each iteration). The `MUTATING_METHODS` fallback catches user methods named like a builtin mutator (push/pop/…); arbitrarily-named ones (compact/bump) stay uncovered. Fix = a conservative name-based over-approx (any known `&self`-mutator method NAME marks its receiver mutated) built from the STABLE `fn_param_ownerships` pre-pass — DEFERRED: clone-balloon risk program-wide (the memory gate must re-confirm) + misses generic-instance sigs registered DURING lowering (the ALREADY-FILED R38 generic-equip residual, ~line 353 — cite, don't re-file). Fixture `known_gaps/cow_loop_bare_param_user_mutator.gg` (`#[ignore]`, asserts 3,2,2,4; Rust lane 3,3,4,4 — **the SH lane's LOOP hoist already gets this CORRECT (3,2,2,4, driver-verified 2026-07-19): a live "reference lags self-host" succession data point**). SCOPE UPDATE (planner-c1 hotfix, 2026-07-19): the class now explicitly includes BRANCH scopes, symmetric-wrong on BOTH lanes by design — the SH branch hoist filters to Rust-prescan-visible shapes via the mirrored `RUST_PRESCAN_MUTATOR_FALLBACK` (lane symmetry; the SH's unilateral richer branch detection was the bootstrap clone-bomb). The class lifts on both lanes together when the Rust prescan learns typed user receivers (a planner-campaign consumer; the stable `fn_param_ownerships` pre-pass is the route, clone-balloon gate applies) — then DELETE `RUST_PRESCAN_MUTATOR_FALLBACK`/`MUTATING_METHODS` mirrors on both sides.

- **[LOW — self-host, Rust; T-B residual, Core #8] name-collision on an UNRESOLVABLE projection stays read-only (both the SCAN and the GATE tail).** The user→builtin order closes the name-collision for a RESOLVABLE receiver (named, or a projection whose root local + field/element types resolve — verified: `v[i].get()` / `s.v[i].get()` materialize). Two unresolvable tails remain, both DEGRADE-TO-BASE (write-through, no bomb): (a) SCAN — in `mutinf_scan_expr`, a self-projection whose type resolves to −1 at scan time (`self.field.get()` where `self.field`→−1) falls to `builtin(get)=Some(false)`; (b) GATE — in `mutinf_recv_type_name`, a projected receiver whose base is NOT a named-root chain (e.g. an element of a METHOD-returned collection `f().v[0].get()`, or a root local absent from `ctx.locals`) resolves to "" → the guard returns false. Fix = a fuller projection-type oracle (or route the scan/gate through the typechecker's per-link `expr_link_types` where present), re-validating RSS since a conservative "unresolved ⇒ mutating" fallback here is the re-bomb the whole pass avoids.
- **[LOW — self-host, Rust; T-B residual] self reborrowed into a local then mutated is not traced by the scan.** `Holder r = self; r.set_name(..)` inside a `&self` method — the scan does not recognise `r` as self-rooted (`mutinf_expr_is_self_rooted("r")` is false), so `r.set_name` is not attributed to self. VERIFIED HARMLESS in the self-host's CURRENT model: a bare `Holder r = self` bind COPIES (r is an independent value — `r.set_name` mutates the copy, not self), so the enclosing method is GENUINELY non-self-mutating → read-only classification is CORRECT, no divergence. The residual only bites IF a future `&`-reborrow-into-local (`auto v = &self.field; v.push(..)`, an aliasing bind that writes through) lands (the `&`-bind write-through itself landed round-38 — T-D flipped `cow_amp_bind_ref`/`cow_amp_bind_ref_field` to MATCH; its scalar-field/element sub-case is the deferred-capability residual filed below); then this path must default to MUTATING (belt-and-braces: the EMutableBorrow arm already flags a direct `&self`/`&self.field` formation as mutating). ⚠ SEPARATE DISCOVERED BUG (Rust, PRE-EXISTING, orthogonal to T-B): `Holder r = self; r.set_name(..)` inside a `&self` method makes **Rust gg PANIC** (`src/main.rs:2434` "gg main thread panicked"); the self-host compiles it and prints the read-only-correct result. Own scout (Rust `&self`→owned-local bind path).
- **[LOW — self-host; T-D adv(c) deferred capability, NOT a regression] scalar/struct-field & element `&`-bind write-through is VALUE-COPIED, not aliased.** T-D's `auto r = &b` / `&b.field` write-through fix (landed, `cow_amp_bind_ref`/`cow_amp_bind_ref_field` MATCH) GATES its bare branch on `inner == EIdentifier`, so a projection of a SCALAR/non-resource field or element (`auto r = &b.scalar_field` / `&b[scalar_i]`) routes to the SAFE value-bind fall-through (a copy) rather than a write-through mut-ptr. This is a DEFERRED CAPABILITY, not a regression (pre-fix it also cloned) — zero corpus occurrences today. Implementing the Ptr write-through for scalar-field/element `&`-binds (resolve the field/element slot address for value-typed fields) is the follow-up. Reference: `lower_stmt.gg` SVarDecl EMutableBorrow intercept.

- **🧩 CoW MATERIALIZATION — durable reference (THE RULE + GROUND TRUTH).**
  - **THE RULE (owner-decided):** *Never implicitly clone except when absolutely necessary for safety.* Order **borrow > move > clone**. An implicit clone is legitimate ONLY when BOTH: (1) the destination must OWN (a real ownership boundary), AND (2) the source can be neither aliased nor moved (lives past this point, OR is a collection element / `.get()` result / view-method result / parameter). Any clone NOT forced by (1)∧(2) is a bug. Explicit-type bindings BORROW (identical to `auto`). For collection-element/view/param sources, ownership-taking is clone-NOT-move. (= the `CLAUDE.md` "Ownership at Consuming Positions" contract.)
  - **GROUND TRUTH (re-cite before acting):** Enforcement = `validate_consume_sites` (`src/ir/validate.rs:2400`) runs UNCONDITIONALLY in `lower_module` (`mod.rs:1892`) and `panic!`s (`:1977`) on the first owning position receiving a borrow with no preceding clone/move ⇒ a MISSING materialization CRASHES, doesn't miscompile; ANY clone-removal must keep it GREEN. Taxonomy: `ImplicitCloneReason` (`src/ir/mod.rs:183`, 11 variants, 39 sites) via `ensure_owned_at_boundary` (`context.rs:1752`), `ensure_owned_at_consuming_arg` (`context.rs:1923`), `emit_enum_init_owned` (`context.rs:1243`) + write-side `cow_before_mutation` (`context.rs:2658`) + a GorgetString-ONLY runtime materialize net. Var-decl tree `lower_var_decl_assign_mode` (`stmts/mod.rs:1081-1273`): A/C→Borrow, F/G→Move, **B/D/E→Clone** (B = cross-type live `Str→GorgetString`; D = CoW-unsafe live; E = `View`→owned String). Clean references: `CLAUDE.md` + `docs/book/11-ownership.md:282-300` + `language-reference.md:2321`/`:1624`.
  - **Branch B is DEAD CODE — not an over-clone (empirically confirmed agent aa18fc8b).** Branch B (`stmts/mod.rs:1170-1179`) fired 0 times across 1121 fixtures — guard `!is_resource_type(rhs) && clone_fn_for_ptr(rhs).is_some()` is the EMPTY SET (every type with a registered clone fn carries `copy_semantics=Resource`). FOLLOW-UP (evidence-first): delete with a tombstone (after confirming no `GG_BACKEND=llvm`/non-fixture path reaches it), OR find the uncovered shape. Do NOT "convert B to Copy".
  - **Branch E over-clone (`stmts/mod.rs:1235-1241`) — RE-VERIFY IT FIRES FIRST.** Flagged (eager clone of a `View`-source same-type `String b = view`) but never confirmed firing. Instrument E + build the corpus to confirm a non-zero count + real over-clone BEFORE any fix. Defer-to-mutation infra exists (`set_view_of`/`views_of_source`/`cow_before_mutation` Case 4); higher risk (past naive view/alias removals regressed 50+ fixtures). Evidence-first.

- **🐛 SELF-HOST MISCOMPILES in-place `dict.get(k).unwrap().push(v)` → SIGSEGV stage-1 binary, CONTEXT-DEPENDENT** (discovered 2026-06-28 by the `implements`-predicate scout). Mutating a `Vector` obtained from a `Dict.get(k).unwrap()` IN PLACE and relying on the dict seeing the mutation (the in-place idiom `gmod.trait_impls_by_type.get(k).unwrap().push(v)`) mis-compiles into a faulting stage-1 binary specifically in the `lower_module` pre-pass context (`tests/fixtures/self_host_lowerer/lower.gg`, the trait-impl-registry build loop). The SAME idiom compiles fine elsewhere in the self-host (`lir_ssa`/`scope`/`lir_codegen`), so it is context-dependent — likely an alias/temp-lifetime interaction between the `Dict.get().unwrap()` view and an in-place `.push()` reallocation in this particular function/scope shape. The `implements` pre-pass DODGES it via rebuild-then-put (`get → owned local → push → put(k, !local)`); the miscompile itself is UNFIXED. Repro: revert the rebuild-then-put in `lower_module`'s `trait_impls_by_type` loop to the in-place idiom and run `self_host_bootstrap_fixed_point` → stage-1 SIGSEGV. Proper fix lives in the self-host lowerer's view-vs-owned / `.get().unwrap()` aliasing path (mirror Rust's treatment); until then keep the rebuild-then-put shape at this site.

- **🐛 SELF-HOST MISLOWERS `None()` (with parens) → mistyped `Option[int]` temp** (#14 P0). The PARSER accepts both `None()` and bare `None` (`self_host_parser/parser.gg:1478`), but the LOWERER lowers a bare-variant CALL `None()` as a constructor defaulting to `Option[int]` regardless of context → mistyped dead temp → stage-1 cc fails (`incompatible types … int64_t from Option__int64_t`). Bare `None` lowers correctly (type flows from context). Rust gg tolerates `None()` → `fixed_point`-only. PROPER FIX = infer `None()`'s Option type from the consuming position (route through the same context-typed None path); OR (lesser) reject `None()` at check time. Likely the same root as `project_selfhost_none_typing_bug`.

- **SELF-HOST lexer bug (NOT a Rust bug): string literal inside an f-string interpolation truncates the enclosing `match`.** A nested string literal in an f-string interpolation (`f"... {f(\"add\", x)} ..."`) is mis-lexed: the `\"` inside `{...}` ends the f-string early → everything after that `case` arm is silently dropped (nasty: no parse error at the site, a downstream non-exhaustive-match error one function over + a phantom extern). The Rust compiler does NOT have this (`src/lexer/mod.rs:691-694` calls `skip_quoted_string` which skips `\\` escapes). Fix the self-host interpolation-segment scanner (`tests/fixtures/self_host_*/lex.gg`) to mirror `skip_quoted_string`. Workaround: bind the inner call to a `String` local first.

#### [DEEP track — NOT single-zone] Full loop-else (for-else all paths + while-else)
Loop-else scout (round-8) proved the full feature is NOT bounded to lower_loops.gg. Ship the zone-clean `lower_for_iterator` slice first (+1 `iterable`); the REST is a dedicated serialized broad track:
- **for-else** across `lower_for_range`(:701)/`lower_for_vector`(:224)/`lower_for_dict`(:491)/`lower_for_string`(:325) — mechanism proven (copy `lower_for_set` lower_loops.gg:606-688: `else_bb=exit_bb; if else_body.len()>0: else_bb=new_block`, header-false/`next()→None` edge → else_bb, break pushes exit_bb so it skips else, emit else tail → jump exit). Centralize via a shared `emit_loop_else_tail(...)` helper. DELETE the dispatcher `lower_fail` guards at lower_loops.gg:176-177 (vector) + :188-189 (dict) as each path gains real handling. SPILLS into `lower_expr.gg` (12 comprehension call-sites: 3×range, 7×vector, 1×dict, 1×string need an empty-`[]` else arg).
- **while-else** = DEEP cross-file AST-arity change: the gap is at the PARSER (`parser.gg:2962-2965` parses then DISCARDS the while-else block), and `SWhile(SpannedExpr, Vector[Stmt])` (`ast.gg:149`) has NO else field. Needs: ast.gg new field + parser bind/emit + ~14 `case SWhile(...)` match sites (meta.gg×4, lower_generics.gg×8, lower_closures.gg×4, lower_cow.gg, resolve.gg×2, typecheck.gg, format.gg, loader.gg×4, lower.gg×2). This is the load-bearing blocker for `for_else` + `break_nested`.
- Rust ref: `src/ir/lowering/stmts/for_loops.rs` `alloc_for_blocks`(:33-44)+`emit_else_arm_tail`(:68-81); `mod.rs:2290-2343` (while dual-target). Docs: `docs/language-reference.md:1276` (else runs on natural exhaustion, break skips it).
- Yield when COMPLETE: `iterable`(+via slice) + `for_else` + `break_nested` = +3 total. Bootstrap-safe (self-host source has zero loop-else).

#### [follow-up, from none_literal review] map_ast_type registration side-effect is a latent footgun
`map_ast_type` calls `lookup_or_register_named` (lower_types.gg:~645) — a REGISTRATION SIDE EFFECT, so it is NOT a pure type-query. The fn_param_sigs pre-pass guard `not fsdef.is_extern_stub` (lower.gg:3550) LOCALLY contains it for stub PARAMS, but the unguarded return-path registration (`map_fn_return_type`→`map_ast_type`, lower.gg:3555) would re-trip it for a future `extern Guard[bool] make()` (a stub whose RETURN is a named resource). Principled fix: split a side-effect-free pure type-query path from the register-on-demand path, OR make the pre-pass use the query-only variant. Not urgent (no current fixture trips the return path), but the footgun is real.

#### [defensive, from Increment-2 review] is_move guard on ByMutRef reclassify
The ByMutRef reclassify (lower_expr.gg:~3186) emits a `MutPtr(&outer_slot)` env field. Escape-unsafe by design (like Rust closures.rs:131) — safe ONLY because Rust gg rejects escaping mutating closures at borrow-check + the parity harness gates on the Rust oracle, so such programs are excluded. The self-host driver OMITS the borrow-check diagnostic (pre-existing, separately-filed gap), so for a Rust-REJECTED escaping mutating closure it would emit dangling-pointer C (vs stub garbage pre-Increment-2). Unreachable from valid Gorget, but defensive hardening = add an `is_move`/escape guard at the reclassify mirroring `closures.rs:102` (only ByMutRef a NON-escaping closure), OR wire the self-host borrow-check diagnostic. Belongs to the borrow-check-diagnostic-gap track.

#### 🆕 DISCOVERED DEFECTS (R5/R6 parity wave, 2026-06-27 — filed, parity-neutral/latent)
- **🐛 ROUND-31 FOLLOW-UP (Core #8) — SELF-HOST semantic-reject port (scope WIDENED round-32).** Round-31 (`79c3ee30`) made the RUST compiler REJECT unknown methods on primitives; the SELF-HOST is a lowerer with NO semantic-reject mechanism (`guess_return_type` name-match; unknown→`I64_TYPE` default, `lower_types.gg`/`lower_closures.gg:838`) → it still SILENTLY accepts what Rust now rejects. The port's reject list has GROWN to three classes, all landed Rust-side in rounds 31-32: (1) unknown primitive methods (round-31); (2) non-int/non-String positional 1-arg `String(x)` ctor args (round-32 `0dc391c6` — self-host currently identity-coerces, which incidentally matches the future cast-via-construction RFC, but the DIVERGENCE stands until the port); (3) definite-return / noreturn-divergence (round-32 `f7c828f3` — self-host accepts non-void fall-off). Port all three behind one mechanism (method/ctor/return-path existence checks against the typed tables). Own scout→brief.
- **🧹 ROUND-31 FOLLOW-UP (Core #1/#3, layering) — the `builtin_method_type` vs IR `GORGET_STRING_VIEW` two-list drift.** Round-31's reject uses `builtin_method_type` (`typecheck.rs:5777`, 50 String methods) as the method-existence oracle, and had to ADD `clone`/`upper`/`lower`/`slice`/`ord` to it because they were IR-protocol-only (`GORGET_STRING_VIEW.methods`, 25) — TWO parallel String-method lists that disagree (the protocol MISSES 31 valid methods `contains`/`split`/`replace`/etc.). The stronger single-oracle fix: have the reject (and return-type inference) consult ONE authoritative registry (the IR protocol, completed) instead of the hand-synced typecheck table. Reconcile the two lists / make one derive from the other. Own scout.

#### [closure 4e-2 — ⚠ NO LONGER "LATENT": MEASURED LIVE, Core #8 silent-wrong-output] Option/Result combinator adapter SKIPS the payload's user `Drop` and LEAKS
**⚠ PREMISE CORRECTED 2026-07-25 by the Track-B2 scout — the "Rust handles it" claim below is REFUTED.**
Measured at HEAD, C backend: `Option[Money].map(...)` with `equip Money with Drop` prints the value but
**NEVER prints "dropping"** — the user `Drop` is silently skipped. That is **silent-wrong-output**
(Core #8), not merely a latent leak. LSan on a 2000-iteration `Option[Vector[int]].map` probe:
**176,000 B in 4,000 allocations**. It is combinator-SPECIFIC — the same program written
`if o is Some(v)` is clean. **D-2T also fails to reject a drop-tainted value at this materialize
position**, so the drop-purity gate does not cover it either. Reproduces at baseline AND under the B2
prototype, i.e. pre-existing and independent of the consume-position migration. Per Core #8 a
pre-existing known defect is NOT a licence to ship past it.
**DURABLE REPRO (committed 2026-07-25):** `tests/fixtures/security/sound_option_map_user_drop_leak.gg`
+ `security.rs::sound_option_map_user_drop_no_leak` (`#[ignore]`d, `security_safe_no_leak`, asserts
the INTENDED `Drop`-runs / no-leak state). RED-verified at HEAD: prints
`512/control-end/dropping/512/map-end` — the trailing `dropping` MISSING — plus
`LeakSanitizer: 8192 bytes in 2 allocations` (`Money__clone` via `Option__Money__clone`, and the
payload). The fixture carries the `if o is Some(v)` CONTROL LEG in the same program, which is clean,
so the map leg's silence is pinned as a divergence rather than a design choice.
⚠ AMBIGUITY recorded in-fixture: the expected stdout asserts ONE `dropping` per leg (the CoW-charter
answer — the combinator's implicit `Option__Money__clone` is itself excess implicit cloning). A fix
that keeps the clone and drops BOTH copies prints `dropping` twice and fails — the intended signal,
not a false red. Out of ggdef's phase-0 subset. **Un-ignore when the combinator adapter owns its
payload.**
**ORIGINAL FILING BELOW — its "LATENT / Rust handles it" framing is superseded:**
The (B) ownership/double-free tail of 4e is LATENT: the whole corpus returns only STATIC-LITERAL Strings (cap=0, .rodata) from map/map_err/unwrap_or_else closures → no heap double-free. It only surfaces with a HEAP String return (e.g. `.map((x): f"{x}")` or `.map((s): s.to_upper())`). Rust handles it (clone-receiver + Move-mode + scrut-zero, methods.rs:2924-2970/:3083-3109). To pursue: (1) ADD a heap-return combinator fixture, (2) port Rust's ownership adapter into the inline-C template/dispatch, (3) ASan-gate (a wrong fix trades the absence-of-crash for a silent double-free — worse). DEFERRED: no corpus fixture currently fails on it, so it's +0 parity until a fixture exists; do it when a heap-return combinator program is added or surfaces. The combinator sub-track (4a/4b/4c/4e-1/4d + R2-class) is otherwise COMPLETE.

#### [nit, non-blocking, from B output-review] `byte` alias on signed arm in resolve_field_gir_type
`resolve_field_gir_type` (lower_types.gg) keeps `byte` on the signed arm (`int8_t|i8|byte→I8_TYPE`), but Rust treats `byte` as a `uint8` alias (`src/lexer/token.rs:471`, `src/ir/lowering/exprs/mod.rs:2566`) and the sibling `prim_name_to_type` (lower_types.gg:56-58) correctly maps `byte→U8_TYPE`. Currently DEAD (field type names reach this fn in canonical `uint8_t` form, not surface `byte`), so not a regression — but tighten for sibling-site consistency: move `byte` to the unsigned `uint8_t|u8|byte→U8_TYPE` arm. Verify no fixture flips before/after (should be a no-op).

#### [micro-guard, from C output-review] range/string for-paths drop else_body SILENTLY
Unlike vector(:177)/dict(:189) which emit a `lower_fail` else-guard, `lower_for_range`(:103) and `lower_for_string`(:206) drop a loop-`else` body with NO marker — so a range/string for-else silently vanishes instead of failing loudly. PART OF the deep loop-else track (those paths get real else-handling there), but as a CHEAP interim ratchet, add a `lower_fail("for-range/string with else-body not yet implemented")` guard to both so the gap can't hide. Do this when picking up the deep track (or as a 2-line standalone).

#### [nit, from D output-review] stale docstring on decide_operand_at_consuming_arg
`lower.gg:~1808` docstring still says "Status: dead code in this commit. No caller exists." — FALSE: the fn is wired via `wire_one_operand` (lower_liveness.gg:970). Pre-existing, comment-only (no codegen impact). Correct the docstring to reflect it's the live CFG-aware wire-pass operand-mode decision. 1-line cleanup.

#### [nit, non-blocking, from throws-expr-body review] Defect-A peel is single-level
`lower_stmt.gg:545` peels `GtPtr`/`GtMutPtr` off `val`'s type ONCE (not recursive like `peel_ptr_tid`). Sufficient today (return-site operands are at most single-Ptr-wrapped Result; green 813-net), but a future borrow-of-borrow Result return would under-peel. If `peel_ptr_tid` is cheap to call there, prefer it for robustness. Inconsequential now.

#### [closure follow-up, from Increment-3 review] closure_value_ret_type channel follow-the-value gap
The Increment-3 `closure_value_ret_type` channel is populated only at the closure make-site + the SVarDecl-MoveDirect copy-forward. A closure value reaching a callable local via a NON-MoveDirect VarDecl branch (CoW/Branch-C), a reassignment (`f = (...)`), or store-in-collection-then-retrieve MISSES the channel → falls back to `expected_type` (same as pre-Increment-3, no regression — just unaddressed). The fn-ARG-then-called path IS handled (GtFnPtr param fires the FnPtr-recovery arm first). Extend the copy-forward to the other VarDecl branches + reassignment when a later increment needs it.

Fix = add a recursion-depth guard (tied to fuel or an explicit depth counter) → `FuelExhausted`/`Trap`, OR soften the RFC §2.2 text and keep this fixture in the RFC-C11 implementation-defined exclusion set (owner call). The two promoted P1-A gates (`spec/ggdef/tests/coverage_histogram.rs`, `converter_agreement.rs`) currently EXCLUDE `stack_guard_deep_recursion.gg` by name + run in a 2 GiB-stack thread to avoid the crash; that exclusion is the workaround this fix retires.
