#### Trap-normalization (D11) T2a-rust follow-ups (filed 2026-07-10 by the T2a-rust executor)
- **[MEDIUM — T1-zone] `spectests/run/trap_shift.gg` conformance fixture (BLOCKED on ggdef modeling shift).** T2a-rust made an out-of-range shift (`x << 64`) trap `T_Overflow` + exit 101 on BOTH Rust backends (C already trapped; the missing LLVM shift-range check was ADDED, owner ruling 2026-07-10). There is currently an integration test pinning both-backend agreement (`tests/integration.rs` shift test) but NO conformance fixture, because ggdef's evaluator (`spec/ggdef/src/eval.rs`) does not yet MODEL a shift operator / its out-of-range trap. To add `trap_shift.gg` (ggdef-adjudicated), first extend ggdef to evaluate `<<`/`>>` and Trap `Overflow` when the count is out of range, then generate the fixture (crosses into T1's zone + shifts MIN_FIXTURES/floors). Keep the closed registry unchanged (shift → existing `T_Overflow`, no new variant).
- **[MEDIUM — T1-zone] `spectests/run/trap_assert_cmp.gg` conformance fixture for the MESSAGE-LESS comparison assert (`assert a == b`).** T2a-rust rerouted BOTH assert forms to `trap[T_AssertFailed]` + exit 101: the message form (via `gorget_trap`) AND the message-less comparison form (via `gorget_assert_fail_values`, now a 4-arg sig carrying the `T_AssertFailed` code — approach (a)). The existing `trap_assert.gg` exercises the MESSAGE form only; the message-less form is currently pinned by integration tests (`assert_fails`, `test_should_panic`, `test_failure`) but not by a conformance fixture. ggdef already models `Stmt::Assert`, so it's generatable — but adding a spectests fixture crosses into T1's zone + shifts MIN_FIXTURES/floors, so do it as a separate small T1 follow-up.
- **[MEDIUM — structural guard] The LLVM `block_exit_labels` twin pre-pass silently drifts from the emit.** `src/backend/llvm/mod.rs:3008` reconstructs each block's exit label (for phi predecessors) by RE-RUNNING the emit's `trap_counter`/block-splitting logic in a SEPARATE match — a hand-maintained twin. T2a-rust's new Shl/Shr shift range-check (which splits the block + bumps `trap_counter`) had to be mirrored there by hand; forgetting it produced `llc: use of undefined value '%ov.*.ok'` that ONLY the self-host bootstrap caught (all trap fixtures + spec_conformance passed). This is a latent class: any future block-splitting `Inst` variant added to `emit_inst` without a matching arm in `block_exit_labels` silently miscompiles under a phi. Two options: (a) a `tests/lints.rs` guard asserting the two match-arm sets agree (the `Inst` variants that bump the counter must be identical in both), or (b) the real fix — have `emit_inst` RECORD each block's exit label into a map as it emits (single source of truth), deleting the twin entirely. Prefer (b). **⚠ NOW ≥2 CONFIRMED INSTANCES + a latent sibling (2026-07-10 — escalate off low-pri):** (1) T2a-rust's Shl/Shr shift check (fixed). (2) **T2b's `gorget_array_get_at` early-return** (`llvm/mod.rs:~4647`) skipped the generic-CallExtern `*trap_counter += 1` the pre-pass counts → 12 conformance fixtures BUILD-FAIL on the LLVM lane (`arr[i]` before an overflow-checked op, e.g. `sum += a[i]` in a loop); output-review caught it, fixed with a manual bump (`feee30d3`). (3) **LATENT, pre-existing:** the newtype-constructor early-return directly below (`llvm/mod.rs:~4658`) ALSO returns without bumping while the pre-pass counts it — builds+runs correctly TODAY but is the same class, one early-return away from a miscompile. (4) **LATENT, from the xhigh review 2026-07-10 (CONFIRMED mechanism):** the `gorget_trap` CallExtern early-return (`llvm/mod.rs:~4625`) bumps `trap_counter` 0/1/2× (once per GorgetString-typed arg via `marshal_cstr`) while the pre-pass counts every CallExtern as exactly 1 — masked ONLY because every current producer ends its block with `Term::Unreachable`; any future mid-block `gorget_trap` emitter (warn-then-continue trap, a pass appending a drop after the call) desyncs the `ov.*.ok` labels → `llc: use of undefined value`. Fix (b) retires ALL of these (incl. the two manual bumps). **⚠ FIX-(b) MEASURED (R-C scout 2026-07-10): NOT drop-in.** The pre-pass is 227 lines, single consumer (`mod.rs:3297`); (b)'s mechanics are ~25 lines (phi placeholders + record `current_label` per block + post-loop patch) MINUS the 227 — but correctness INVERTS to "every label-creating site must update `current_label`", and at least one already violates it: `gorget_str_clear` creates `scl.done` labels and returns WITHOUT updating (`mod.rs:5356-5361`; the pre-pass covers it at `:3238`). ~44 label-writing sites need that audit; the FULL LLVM sweep is the only honest gate. Own track, bootstrap-gated; instance count when it runs: 2 fixed + 2 latent + the R-C guard's mirrored arm to retire.
- **[LOW — cosmetic, not conformance-compared] Message-form `assert` trap line renders `at <unknown>:0:0`.** T2a-rust reroutes assert-with-message to `gorget_trap(T_AssertFailed, msg)` → `gorget_trap_at` threading the emit-site span (like `panic`). The code + exit 101 are correct and BYTE-IDENTICAL across C/LLVM, but the source LOCATION on the assert trap line resolves to `<unknown>:0:0` (Q-D wanted a real span). Root cause is a PRE-EXISTING GIR→LIR span-propagation gap for these branch-target trap blocks — the old `gorget_panic` assert emitted `<unknown>:0:0` too, and `condition.span` IS a real byte range (verified), yet the LIR instruction carries no span (both `call_extern` and `call_void` reproduce it, so it is NOT the Call/CallExtern distinction). Conformance does NOT compare the location (impl-defined, Q1), so this is cosmetic. Fix belongs in the GIR→LIR span-map plumbing for branch-target blocks, not trap normalization.

#### D23-throws smith follow-ups (filed 2026-07-10 by the T3b executor)
- **[MEDIUM] 🎲 T3c — a POSITIVE throws DIFFERENTIAL smith tier.** T3b (LANDED, see DONE.md) is the NEGATIVE/rejection tier: `GG_SMITH_TIER=1` generates programs well-formed EXCEPT for ONE unhandled `throws` call and asserts production REJECTS with `E_UnhandledThrows` (an inverted, CHECK-ONLY oracle — `classify_throws` in `tests/smith/main.rs`, `program_throws_positions` in `tests/smith/generator.rs`). T3c is the complement and is arguably HIGHER-value: generate programs with HANDLED throws (auto-propagate inside a `throws` fn, `catch`/`rethrow`, `Result[T,E]` capture, the §10.3 capture forms) that COMPILE, then run the FULL backend differential (production `gg check`→C build/run, ggdef in-process, self-host, LLVM) exactly like tier 0. ggdef already MODELS handled throws (`Propagate`, §10.3 capture, `spec/ggdef/src/elaborate/mod.rs`), so it can adjudicate — this would catch a backend MISCOMPILING the throws→`Result` desugar at RUNTIME (a class the negative tier structurally cannot reach, since those programs never compile). It is a NEW tier number (not `TIER_THROWS=1`) reusing tier 0's differential `classify` path, so no new oracle shape — the work is the generator grammar (a `throws`-helper set + the handling forms) plus a tier dispatch arm. Own scout→brief→≥3 reviews before launch (the generator MUST emit programs that are well-formed AND actually handle the throws — the false-positive discipline from T3b's scout applies in mirror image).
- **[LOW] 🎲 T3b equip-method receiver-position extension (deferred from T3b).** T3b's generator ships the free-fn `risky()` form only; the `equip`-method receiver position (`s.m()` where `int m(self) throws String:`) was OMITTED because it needs a synthesized `struct S` + in-scope instance + `equip S:` block to be well-formed, and the scout found the false-positive trap that a bare `int risky(self) throws String:` WITHOUT `equip` is ACCEPTED (`self` parses as a plain param) — a mis-resolving shape would mis-fire the inverted oracle as a spurious slip. T3a's `d23_unhandled_method{,_traitdefault,_xmod}` fixtures already cover the method-reject, so this is incremental hardening, not a gap. If added: spell out + verify the struct+instance+equip scaffolding produces a well-formed-except-for-the-one-throws program before wiring it as a new position in `emit_throws_position`.
#### Trap-normalization (D11) T2a-selfhost follow-ups (filed 2026-07-10 by the T2a-selfhost executor)
- **[MEDIUM — self-host-shift-parity, Core-#8] The self-host emits an UNGUARDED out-of-range shift — the lone backend with silent UB on `x << 64`.** T2a-rust made an out-of-range shift trap `T_Overflow` + exit 101 on BOTH Rust backends (C already trapped; the LLVM shift-range check was added, owner ruling 2026-07-10). The self-host lowerer does NOT guard shift at all: `tests/fixtures/self_host_lowerer/lir_codegen.gg:4536` (IShl) / `:4539` (IShr) emit raw `v = lhs << rhs;` / `>> ` with no range check, so a self-host-compiled `x << 64` is silent UB where both Rust backends now trap. This is PRE-EXISTING (the self-host never guarded shift) and NOT in the conformance corpus (ggdef does not model shift yet — see the T2a-rust `trap_shift.gg` item above), so it does NOT affect the 7-fixture flip and it is NOT a "reroute an existing guard" (there is no guard to reroute — it needs a NEW one). Fix: add the self-host shift-range guard emitting `gorget_trap("T_Overflow", "shift out of range")` (mirroring the Rust C emit `src/backend/c_lir/mod.rs:2649/2656`: `if ((uint64_t)rhs >= (uint64_t)(sizeof(ct)*8)) { gorget_trap(...); }`) + a self-host-lane shift test, so all three backends agree. Keep the closed registry unchanged (shift → existing `T_Overflow`, no new variant).

### Low

- **🆕🧹 [MED-LOW — typed-metadata smell in OUR OWN test infra, self-filed 2026-07-16] The ggdef corpus out-of-subset mechanism is TWO name-keyed EXCLUDE lists (`corpus_b.rs` + `corpus_b1.rs`) — sibling drift by construction** (the 1B fixture needed both edited; 1C will again; a missed twin = a bricked lane, exactly the parallel-list class rule 2 forbids). Reference-grade shape: ONE typed frontmatter key on the fixture itself (e.g. `# ggdef-subset: false` + reason) read by both corpus harnesses — set at the source, no name lists. Small slice; retires both EXCLUDE lists.
- **🧹 [P1-B review follow-up, 2026-07-06] ggdef frontmatter reader: unknown TOP-LEVEL keys with nested/`|`-block values error (`MalformedKey`) instead of being tolerated.** Loud, never silent — no seed trips it today — but RFC §4's `files:` is a future block-shaped key. Fix: make the `other` arm (`spec/ggdef/src/frontmatter.rs:192` at filing) consume-and-discard an unknown key's indented children. Doc-comments were narrowed to inline-only at integration (module doc LIMIT note cites this entry).

- **🧹 [P1-B review follow-up, 2026-07-06] ggdef frontmatter `Expect{exit,stdout}` is RUN-TIER-ONLY — not the all-tier contract.** RFC §4 static-error/parse-error tiers express `expect:` as a diagnostic code (`expect: code: E_…`, no exit/stdout). The tier-track that populates those directories must refactor `Expect` into a tier-discriminated enum (breaking change to the 4-lane-shared type, `frontmatter.rs:41-46` at filing) — do not treat the run-tier shape as final.

- **🧹 [ggdef hardening, from P1-A pass-2 review 2026-07-06; PRE-EXISTING, unreachable via `gg check`] Closure bodies inherit the enclosing fn's `current_fn_throws`.** A closure body containing a bare throws call is silently evaluated by ggdef (garbled `ok Error(neg)` on the throwing path) where production REJECTS at typecheck (`E_TypeMismatch`) — so no check-accepted program reaches it, but the loudness invariant should hold unconditionally. Fix: scope/reset `current_fn_throws` (and capture_ctx state) at closure boundaries, or make unmodeled closure-throws a loud ElabError. spec/ggdef elaborate/mod.rs.

- **🧹 [P1-G follow-up] Extend diagnostic codes to lex/parse time.** `LexErrorKind`/`ParseErrorKind` still render bare `error:` (noted in `spec/prose/diagnostic-codes.md` §Follow-up). Same recipe as P1-G: catch-all-free `code()` per enum, thread at the reporter, register in the prose table.

#### Trap-normalization (D11) T2b follow-ups (filed 2026-07-10 by the T2b executor)
- **[LOW — reference-grade span plumbing; not conformance-compared] The RARER bounds-class sites still trap `trap[T_Bounds] at <unknown>:0:0` (span-less) — thread real `file:line:col` into them, mirroring the flagship `gorget_array_get_at`.** T2b gave the flagship Vector element READ (`v[i]`) a REAL source location via `gorget_array_get_at(arr, idx, code, file, line, col)` + an emit-boundary reroute on BOTH Rust backends (`src/backend/c_lir/emit_call_extern.rs`, `src/backend/llvm/mod.rs`), but the rest of the index/bounds class stays SPAN-LESS (owner ruling: flagship-only in T2b): the runtime helpers `gorget_array_set/remove/insert/swap/swap_remove/slice`, `gorget_str_index/slice/byte_slice/byte_at`, `gorget_shared_array_get/set`, and the ~16 `gorget_bytes_*` offset checks all call the span-less `gorget_trap(GG_T_BOUNDS, detail)` → `trap[T_Bounds]: … at <unknown>:0:0` + exit 101 (correct code + exit; only the LOCATION is `<unknown>`). Conformance does NOT compare the location (impl-defined, D11 Q1), so this is reference-grade polish, not a correctness gap. Fix = per-site `_at` variants (arr/idx/…, code, file, line, col) + an emit-boundary reroute for each of the `v[i]=x` write / `.remove` / `.insert` / `.slice` / `str[i]` / `shared[i]` / bytes-offset call sites, exactly as `gorget_array_get_at` did for the read. This is the same per-runtime-fn span-plumbing as the broader runtime-side item (see the "Runtime-side panic locations (~50+ sites)" entry) — could fold into it. Pairs with self-host span-threading (`<unknown>:0:0` there too, existing LOW entry).

#### ====== REFERENCE-GRADE REJECTION TRACK (owner-asked 2026-06-27; audit @ 65c2cdc0) ======
⚠⚠ OWNER STEER 2026-06-27 (TWO-PHASE): **PHASE 1 (NOW, until 100% parity)** = PARITY is PRIMARY; do bounded single-node rejections OPPORTUNISTICALLY only (FP-enumerable, parity-neutral; they seed the negative corpus). **PHASE 2 (AFTER 100% parity)** = build the GENERAL type-enforcement pass (explicit-VarDecl type-mismatch → un-ignores `self_host_check_rejects_illtyped`; #6 TYPE-mismatch ~42-`self.error`-site migration; #8 borrow-check port). DEFERRED-NOT-DECLINED — the enforcement pass is worth doing, just AFTER parity (it's parity-orthogonal + reads more-trustworthy types once inference is maximally correct + the dominant risk is FP). So in PHASE 1: do NOT start #6/#7/#8 or the VarDecl-mismatch as a track; only land a rejection if it's bounded + FP-enumerable.
SELF-HOST IS TOO PERMISSIVE: Rust gg rejects 116 fixtures, self-host ACCEPTS 109 (emits C). Self-host typecheck is an INFERENCE pass (~6 control-flow diagnostics only), NO general type/arg/trait/const enforcement, NO borrow/move/safety pass. Parity-NEUTRAL (rejected fixtures are RUST-REJECTED, excluded from denom) — pure correctness wins per invariant #8.
TEST MECHANISM (exists, under-used): `check_gg_fails` (integration.rs:6486) = Rust rejects; `self_host_driver_rejects_invalid_program` (integration.rs:16510, runs driver `--lir-c`, asserts non-zero exit + codespan + EMPTY stdout) = self-host rejects. Clone the latter per gap. (The hole: c_emit_comparison:15182 + self_host_runtime_diff:17897 `return RustRejected` without running the self-host.)
★ EVERY rejection MUST gate on: 1208-Rust-accepted-fixture FP sweep (0 false positives) + `self_host_bootstrap_fixed_point` (a new reject that trips the self-host's OWN 667K-line source breaks bootstrap) + `type_comparison`.
WRITE SITE: self_host_typechecker/typecheck.gg (SYMLINKED into self_host_lowerer → affects build/check + type_comparison + bootstrap).
LANDING ORDER (most-bounded first; items 1/2/3a are in DONE.md):
3b. wrong_arg_count / wrong_field_count (signature via infer_expr_type/scope) — ⚠ wrong_arg_count is ENTANGLED with the filed 🐛 Rust-gg method-default-arg-fill defect (Rust mis-counts method calls that omit a defaulted arg); resolve that defect FIRST, or scope 3b to free-function arg-count only.

#### [rejection follow-up, invariant #8 — from Rejection-#1 review] positional-after-named on METHOD calls accepted by BOTH compilers
Rust gg's `check_named_args_and_defaults` (PositionalAfterNamed) is invoked at ONLY the free-function ECall path (typecheck.rs:1569), NOT for method calls — Rust gg ACCEPTS `s.compute(a=1, 2)` (exit 0), and so does the self-host. So Rejection #1 (self-host ECall, not EMethodCall) faithfully mirrors Rust's CURRENT scope. But per invariant #8 this is a ≥2-bug defect BOTH compilers share: reject positional-after-named on METHOD calls in Rust gg (extend the check to the EMethodCall path) AND the self-host (add the same walk to the EMethodCall typecheck arm) + a negative fixture asserting both reject. Separate follow-up.

- **🎨 CAST-VIA-CONSTRUCTION LANGUAGE REDESIGN (owner-approved DESIGN 2026-06-20; impl deferred) — RFC `the cast-via-construction RFC (git history)`. ⚠ §7+§8 are the AUTHORITATIVE resolution layer (supersede §3–§6); read those, not the old §3–§4 sketch.** Retire `as`, `Into`, `TryFrom`, `TryInto`: conversion IS construction. `T(x)` is the sole spelling; the `From` registry survives UNDER THE HOOD (a 1-arg ctor `Self(T)` auto-registers it — `From` = the capability name + the generic-bound name, §7.4 Seam B). Fallibility = the `throws` effect, TYPE-level (widening ctor = non-`throws`, narrowing = `throws`) — NO value-flow analysis. **RESOLVED 2026-06-20 (§7.3):** (1) narrowing **overflow → THROW** (recoverable; off the strict-panic stance) — arithmetic overflow is a FAULT not a contract error, see error-model doc; (2) **CORRECTED 2026-06-22:** flavors + float→int modes are **NAMED-ARG CONSTRUCTORS** (`byte(clamping=x)`/`int(truncating=f)`/`int(rounding=f)`) — type=callee, mode=static label (Swift's `UInt8(clamping:)`); the 2026-06-20 source-method detour (`x.clamped(byte)`) was REVERSED (it conflated a static arg-LABEL with a runtime mode-PARAM AND put a type in value-arg position); (3) value→String is **`String(T)`** (= `Displayable.display()`, infallible), `str(x)` stays rejected. **Seams (§7.4):** float→int has no bare `int(f)` form (mode-labeled construction `int(rounding=f)` etc.); generics relocate the From/TryFrom distinction to bound-form (not deleted). **§8:** 9-language survey — Into/Try* dissolve (Into = From-backwards; TryFrom only exists because Rust's From can't fail — a `throws` ctor IS the "Try"); no silent-lossy default (5-language lesson); the `throws` annotation = C#'s implicit/explicit (no new keyword). Remaining work = implementation only; both-compilers + spec + book + negative-fixtures, staged per §6; the EAs `as` fix (parity #1) built the reusable `GICast`→LIR machinery. Own scout→brief→reviews when funded.
- **🧭 ERROR-MODEL LANGUAGE DIRECTION (owner brainstorm 2026-06-20; DRAFT — SCOUT + 3 SEQUENTIAL REVIEWS DONE, clean SIGN OFF `999a54a1`; several owner decisions folded) — `the error-model design notes (git history)`.** Spawned by the cast RFC's arithmetic-overflow sub-decision. Model: value channel + a typed error channel; error TYPES classified **contract** (parse/IO/conversion — mandatory-handle, on the API surface) vs **fault** (overflow/bounds/div0/OOM). Core argument = the IMPOSSIBILITY (§3): recoverable-default-overflow + informative-row + universal-typed = pick two → faults MUST be a non-contract kind. **OWNER DECISIONS (2026-06-21):** (1) **§0.5 reversal ACCEPTED** but ADDITIVE — overflow STAYS panic-by-default (safety rationale intact for the uncaught case); we ADD opt-in recovery, so docs get "panics by default AND catchable," not a full overturn. (2) **Q14 RESOLVED** — faults = a structured closed **`Fault` enum** (never String), the "typed/untyped" framing was a conflation of value-repr (A) / in-signature (B) / static-exhaustive-catch (C); decided A=enum, B=out-of-signature, C=dynamic-match. (3) **§9.1 phased (BOTH, PHASED)** — TYPING vs PROPAGATION are orthogonal; **PHASE 1** = `Fault equip Error` (unified `catch` for both kinds) + panic-by-default + **LOCAL-only catch** (`(a*b) catch Overflow: …` lowers to a checked-op-branch-to-handler = the existing `--overflow=checked` op, no unwind, no ubiquity) + exhaustiveness-via-implicit-panic-default; **⭐ Phase 1 sidesteps EVERY unwind-dependent blocker** (B2 greenfield-unwind, Q9 drop-across-unwind, Q15 FFI, Q16 main-boundary, §3.1 partial-state — all PHASE 2). **PHASE 2** = deep/boundary (server-keeps-serving) catch, needs the greenfield unwind infra, strictly additive over Phase 1. Precedents: Zig/Koka/Swift-6/Midori/Erlang/Java-checked (all accurate per scout). **Open (§9 Q1–Q16 + §9.1):** the fast knob (debug-checked/release-wrap), `meta`-overflow three-way split (wraps silently today `meta.rs:1278-1280`), `Never` spelling, Result reconciliation, full fault set, self-host parity. **§1/§4 "one channel/two kinds" framing KEPT (owner-decided 2026-06-21, "minimal clarify"); a §4 clarification + neutralized-forward-notes landed `4a785ef8`.** Touches the WHOLE language — Phase 1 is the small, shippable increment; the self-host (wall-to-wall arithmetic) needs a parity story first. **PHASE-1 SPEC (§11) REVIEWED 2026-06-21: 4 fresh SEQUENTIAL passes → clean SIGN OFF (`29f6357b`); briefable.** Central impl item = a **NEW LIR checked-faultable-op-with-handler-branch shape in BOTH backends** (no existing trap to re-point — all checks trap inline at C/LLVM emit, after drop passes; model the branch as GIR/LIR CFG like `lower_catch_expr` so drops stay correct). Phase-1 faults = Overflow+DivByZero (inline) + Bounds via `gorget_array_safe_get`+NULL-branch (brief decides Phase 1 vs 1.5); OOM→Phase 2. Also: `Fault equip Error` (3 synth methods display/debug/source), closed-`Fault` scrutinee + panic-default match rule, catch-on-non-throwing = NEW AST/grammar/typecheck form (don't perturb contract `catch`), per-expr overflow-mode override, handler-bb constructs the `Fault` variant. **FINAL pre-impl gate PASSED 2026-06-21 (`a9b212c7`): plan+code review + self-host scout + a confirming pass → clean SIGN OFF, IMPLEMENTATION-READY for both backends.** Final review caught + fixed a stale contradiction (§3.1/§9 Q1 asserted blanket "never inline" — now carved out: boundary-only = Phase-2 deep; Phase-1 local catch is safe, branch-before-store). **SELF-HOST (§11.8, scout-verified): NO prerequisites to fix — the key dependency is already satisfied (self-host emits CHECKED arithmetic today, per-instruction overflow field, `lower_catch_expr` CFG template, enum/trait construction all present); Rust-first regresses NO self-host gate (own source keeps panic-default); self-host fast-follows with the SAME new LIR shape + a greenfield fault-catch AST/typecheck path (`infer.gg` has no ECatch handler) + the `map_binop` unknown-op→OP_ADD footgun to guard.** Sequencing (§11.7): shared LIR shape FIRST → one backend → typecheck → second backend → fixtures; the overflow-mode override is ADDITIVE (don't mutate the `overflow_wrap` global). **ERROR-MODEL — SUPERSEDED BY DECISION BATCH 5 (2026-07-11 ledger LOG): the fault-catch frontier is CLOSED** — D25 removes fault-catch entirely (self-host Inc2, `Fault equip Error`, Phase-2 deep/boundary catch all cancelled; deep catch rejected on the merits, boundary containment = D24's Task-join `TaskFault`, OOM rides D24/phase-3; the fault-participation machinery is deleted by the Batch-C2 track). **SURVIVING open §9 Qs (unaffected by the fault redesign, each still needs an owner ruling eventually):** the fast knob (debug-checked/release-wrap), `meta`-overflow split (`meta.rs:1278` — partly covered by D18), `Never` spelling, Result reconciliation.

- **[MED — SH lane, pre-existing subset gap found by the D31 scout] The SH typechecker has NO call-site ownership check AT ALL** — no `check_call_ownership` equivalent, no DkOwnershipMismatch: it rejects NEITHER free-fn nor method sigil mismatches (`typecheck.gg` has DkMoveWithoutOperator/DkLocalBorrowBind/DkBorrowConflict but nothing comparing `CallArg.ownership` to declared param modes; `infer.gg:171` reads RTFunction ownerships and never compares). **The rule the SH must implement when built = D31 ADDENDUM-2 FULL STRICT (`decisions.md` 2026-07-20, `7a840ae3`): the arg sigil equals the declared param mode at every call site — free-fn AND method, named place or temporary — bare=borrow, `&`=write-through, `!`=consume** (production `check_call_ownership` + `check_method_call_ownership`, `src/semantic/safety/helpers.rs`, both landed 2026-07-20). Implementation discipline: the self-offset is by NAME (`param_names[0]=="self"`, NOT a fixed +1 — extern methods omit self). The whole corpus incl. the migrated self-host is already full-strict-clean, so the check should land at zero migration cost — a Core-#9 lagging-lane note until then.

#### 🆕 D31 FULL-STRICT FOLLOW-UPS (filed 2026-07-20 by the D31 ADDENDUM-2 executor; the full-strict flip itself LANDED — record in DONE.md)
- **[MED — DX tooling, the ADDENDUM-2 rider] `gg fmt`-adjacent auto-insertion of the missing call-site sigil.** The `E_OwnershipMismatch` message is now kind-aware + carries the exact-span fix ("add `!` here" / "add `&` here" / "remove the `!`"), so the edit is machine-actionable. Build the auto-fix: a `gg fmt --fix-ownership` (or an LSP code action) that reads the diagnostic's primary-label span + direction and inserts/removes the sigil. The message infrastructure (`errors.rs` OwnershipMismatch typed modes + `arg_is_temp`; the primary-label hint in `src/errors.rs report_semantic_error`) is the data source. Own small track.
- **[LOW — pre-existing, zero blast radius, Core #8-adjacent] `&` on a TEMPORARY into a `&` param is accepted but is a dead write.** Probed 2026-07-20: production ACCEPTS `f(&Tok(1))` / `c.m(&Ctor())` (found==expected==MutableBorrow, so the strict check passes) — but a write-through to a temporary has no owner to observe it (a dead write). The ledger's ADDENDUM-2 rider expects a REJECT. Blast radius is ZERO (grep of corpus+lib+self-host: 0 `&<ctor/call>` sites), so it was FILED rather than landed with the flip (adding a new reject class + message + NEG + ggdef mirror for a zero-site pre-existing acceptance was out of the flip's scope). Recommendation: reject in `check_call_ownership` + `check_method_call_ownership` when `found==MutableBorrow && !expr_is_place(arg)`, with a "cannot mutably borrow a temporary — bind it to a local first" message; mirror in ggdef; one NEG fixture. NOT introduced by D31 (pre-existing).

  - **(b3) builtin collection/channel methods absent** — `Vector.zip/flat_map/count/find/fill/each/swap/swap_remove/to_string`, `Channel.send/recv/recv_timeout/close/capacity`, `Set.insert/size`, `Dict.size`. Add to the resolution surface.
  - **(c) auto-derivable on user types** — already exempted by `is_auto_derivable`, keep.
  - **ARITY** (`v.push(1,2,3)` panics at `src/lir/validate.rs:137`) — `WrongArgCount` already wired for resolved methods (`typecheck.rs:~1846`). Add arity to builtin method decls + convert the `lir/validate.rs` arity panic (`:253`/`:137`) into a clean `WrongArgCount`. Independent; can ship separately.
  - **`via`-forwarded methods** NOT resolved by `resolve_method` (`traits.rs:157`) — exempted via `has_inherent_only_impls` returning false when `via_field.is_some()` (`traits.rs:~322`). Phase-2 totality needs real `via` resolution.
  - **Safe order:** (1) enumerate builtin methods into the checker (b2+b3) — give `builtin_method_type` a "exists but type unknown" return or a `builtin_method_exists` set; (2) make `error_id` receivers stay silent (b1); (3) resolve `via`; (4) ONLY THEN widen the gate. `self_host_runtime`/`bootstrap_fixed_point` are the canaries (go red on `String.clone` alone).

- **🐛 Self-host lowerer driver OMITS a borrow-check pass** (scout agent af99281f). Pipeline is parse→resolve→typecheck→lower→validators — NO borrow-check. A move-then-use of a named local that Rust correctly REJECTS (`use of moved value`) is SILENTLY lowered to a leaking MOVE (LSan-confirmed; latent UAF), no diagnostic. The CoW *lowering* is correct (the `Some(name.clone())` idiom is language-MANDATED, must STAY). The gap is purely the MISSING DIAGNOSTIC GATE: port/wire a borrow-check pass into the self-host driver. Only bites invalid programs (lower urgency than a miscompile).

- **Safety-pass branch-divergence audit (Cluster C, post-Snag-#39 audit). Defer.** Snag #39's fix added `save_branch_state`/`restore_branch_state` around `Expr::Catch`/`Expr::Rethrow`. No other actively-leaking sites (`DefaultOp`/`And/Or`/`OptionalChain` checked clean — `self.diverged` is only set by `Return/Throw/Break/Continue`). If divergence tracking is extended to noreturn-call expressions (with `panic`/`exit` typed-Never work), audit these. Also consider upgrading Catch/Rethrow from save+restore to save+merge.

- **`caller_location()` builtin + multi-frame stack walking [LOW].** (a) a `#[track_caller]`-like attribute / implicit `caller_location()` so helpers inherit the call site without manual `line, col` plumbing (typed sidecar on `CallExtern`/`Call` carrying caller span, read by the builtin). (b) full backtrace needs DWARF/libunwind in the C backend or LLVM stackmap; async/spawned-task panic locations need the spawning call's span carried through spawn context. Both multi-week; defer until v1 is battle-tested.

- **Result→T auto-propagation — retire the residual consumer-side `maybe_auto_propagate` safety nets.** Producer-side centralization done (`lower_expr` auto-applies on Call/MethodCall + typechecker gates for if/while/elif/index; Snag #49 holdouts closed). The 7 remaining consumer-side calls (`stmts/assigns.rs:112`, `stmts/mod.rs:129/317/1422`, `calls.rs:151/1030/1065/1206`, `methods.rs:224`, `exprs/mod.rs:1788/1823/1854`) exist for the *Identifier-of-Result* case (`Result[T,E] r = ...; T x = r;`) — the producer hook can't fire on identifiers without breaking `body_r.unwrap()` (the receiver MUST stay `Result`). Principled fix = plumb the typechecker's `expr_types` (post-auto-prop type per span) through monomorphization into IR-lowering, check `op_type` vs `expr_types[span]` at `lower_expr` exit, auto-prop only on disagreement in the throws-sugar direction → retire all 7. Estimate 1-2 days (mostly the monomorphization span-plumbing).

- **🆕🐛 [MED — module-loader leniency, found by the CoW-1A output-review probe 2026-07-17] Importing ANY name from a module silently exposes ALL of that module's names.** `lower_expr.gg` called `for_iterable_mode` (defined in `lower_loops.gg`) WITHOUT it appearing in the `from lower_loops import …` list, and the build succeeded — the loader resolves unimported names once any name from the module is imported. Accepts what the import system implies should be rejected (an unimported-name use should be `E_UndefinedName`); it also lets import lists silently rot. Fix in the loader/resolver's import-visibility check + a negative fixture; sweep existing `.gg` sources for other unimported-name uses that the leniency currently masks (each is a latent build break). The 1A remediation added the missing import explicitly.

- **🆕 [D10(b) self-root follow-up, filed 2026-07-14 — OWNER QUESTION] For-loop iterator-invalidation is ROOT-granular for BOTH self and locals — adopt field-path disjointness?** `check_expr.rs:501` (`for_loop_iterables.contains(&recv_def_id)`) compares only the ROOT DefId, discarding the projection path — so `for x in c.a: c.b.push(…)` over-rejects DISJOINT fields (unlike the projection-aware call-arg check `check_call_aliasing`, which uses `find_root_def_id_with_path` + `paths_overlap`). Affects `self` AND identifier roots IDENTICALLY (the self-root fix made self consistent with this pre-existing local behavior, not worse). Should the for-loop check adopt the same field-path disjointness (reuse `find_root_def_id_with_path` + `paths_overlap`) so `for x in c.a: c.b.push()` is accepted? Own scout; measure the in-repo blast radius first (self-root scout found 0 in-repo `for … in self.<field>` sites — all iterate method-call results).
- **🆕 [D10(b) self-root follow-up, filed 2026-07-14 — OWNER QUESTION] Moving a field out of a bare (borrowed) `self` (`take(!self.items)`) stays accepted.** A partial-move-out-of-borrowed-self case — related to the D12/partial-move family and the undecided destructuring-partial-move widening (2026-07-11 D10(a) ADDENDUM). Separate from the self-root place-overlap fix; needs an owner ruling on whether/how partial self-moves are checked. Own scout when the partial-move family is worked.
- **🆕 [D10(b) B1 follow-up, filed 2026-07-14] Disjoint-sibling MOVE `f(!m.a, !m.b)` over-rejects — undecided partial-move widening.** Moving two DISJOINT sibling sub-places today trips `E_UseAfterMove` because the move-tracker root-marks the whole DefId `m` on the first `!m.a`. Whether this SHOULD accept (Rust-style destructuring partial move) is the WIDENING left UNDECIDED by the 2026-07-11 D10(a) ADDENDUM (`decisions.md`). D10(b) place-overlap deliberately keeps `(Move,Move)` out (E_DoubleMove/E_UseAfterMove own it), so this is NOT B1's call. Fixture documents the question: `tests/fixtures/place_overlap_disjoint_sibling_move_error.gg` + the `#[ignore]`d test `place_overlap_disjoint_sibling_move_error`. Needs an OWNER RULING; if widening is ruled in, flip the fixture to a build+run POSITIVE and relax the projection-move root-marking to path-precise.
- **🆕 [D23/T3a follow-up, filed 2026-07-10, MEDIUM-design] General must-use on `Result`.** D23/T3a pins ONLY that an unhandled `throws` call in a CONSUME position rejects (`E_UnhandledThrows`). It does NOT catch a `Result` VALUE that is captured then ignored — `auto r = throws_call()` bound but never matched, or a `Result`-returning fn's value discarded even inside a `throws` fn. Whether Gorget wants a general `#[must_use]`-style must-use-on-`Result` (warn or error on a dropped `Result`) is a separate language-design question — deliberately OUT of D23 scope (owner Q4 ruling). Design + owner-decide before implementing; pairs with the error-model residuals.
- **⚖️ [language-design question, from P1-A §10.3 fold 2026-07-06 — route to decisions.md open queue at the next owner batch] Return-position §10.3 capture asymmetry.** Block-body `return throws_call()` into a Result-declared return type CAPTURES (production), but the IDENTICAL expr-body tail is REJECTED (`type mismatch: expected Result[…], found int` — probes g/f3, durable at /tmp/recover_p1a_r1probe/). ggdef mirrors production (loud ElabError on the expr-body shape). Decide: expr-body should capture too (uniformity — an expr-body is sugar for `return <expr>`) or reject is correct; whichever is ratified → fix production + ggdef + language-reference §10.3 write-through + fixtures.

- **🐛 [SILENT MISCOMPILE — D20 RATIFIED 2026-07-06, ruling settled: reject with the f-string fix-it; fold with the to_string fix-it Low entry + the language-design.md:80 doc fix into ONE small D20 track] `String(3)` check-passes, builds, and prints an EMPTY string** — but `docs/language-reference.md:3224` says `String(value)` IS a compile-time error ("use an f-string"). The `to_string` rejection is complete but this sibling constructor path slipped through. Fix: reject `String(<non-string>)` at check with the f-string fix-it (same class as the A7 batch-4 item). Probes: /tmp/recover_scout_resources/q4e,q4g.

- **🧹 [round-33 alloc scout filing — DIRECTION RATIFIED 2026-07-06 as D13 TWO-STEP (decisions.md LOG): step 1 = REJECT bare allocator locals at check + docs rewrite, briefable now (resources-scout evidence /tmp/recover_scout_resources/); step 2 = full RAII drop-registration w/ borrow_deps ordering, filed target. The 'Decide:' below is DECIDED.] Bare (non-`with`) allocator locals are never destroyed — guaranteed leak class.** `Arena a = Arena(4096)` outside `with` is lowered via plain `call_extern` (no drop registration, `calls.rs` allocator-ctor branches) and there is no scope-exit destroy → ASan LeakSanitizer reports 80B handle + 4096B block (regenerate: bare-arena probe with either Vector or String `alloc=`; identical shape both). PRE-EXISTING for collections (the docs' own §15.3 `alloc=` example uses a bare PoolAllocator). Also NOTE: at arena_depth 0 the escape gate doesn't track bare-allocator-backed values (`check_stmt.rs:194` gates on `arena_depth > 0`), so today the leak is what makes the bare idiom "safe". Decide: drop-register allocator handles (destroy at scope exit + extend escape tracking to bare allocators) or document the leak as the bare-idiom contract. **ESCALATION (round-33 allocator-class scout, 2026-07-02, ASan-verified): not just a leak — manual `.destroy()` of a bare allocator BEFORE scope exit while an RAII value backed by it is still live is a heap-UAF from safe code.** `Arena a = Arena(4096)` + `String s = String("…long…", alloc=a)` + `a.destroy()` → the scope-exit drop of `s` frees through the destroyed arena → ASan heap-use-after-free in `gorget_string_free` (the plain run prints fine — silent). The decide-branch must also cover destroy ordering: drop-registration with ordering fixes both; merely documenting the leak contract does NOT fix this one. (The round's `allocator_ctor_alloc_keyword.gg` fixture uses the with-idiom specifically to sidestep this.)

- **🐛 LATENT: `is_collection_assignment` accepts ANY array/set literal regardless of ELEMENT type.** `is_collection_assignment` (`src/semantic/typecheck.rs:3800`) returns true for any `ResolvedType::Array(_,_)` against a `Vector`/`Dict`/`Set`/`HashMap`/`HashSet` dest WITHOUT checking the element type → `Vector[int] v = ["a", "b"]` + `Vector[int] f(): return ["a", "b"]` both pass `gg check` then misbehave. Pre-existing in VarDecl; the Return/Assign extension inherits the laxity (doesn't introduce it). Fix = unify (or check-compatible) the literal's element type against the dest's declared element type. One helper; affects VarDecl + Return + Assign.

- **`Ordinal` built-in trait never registered → wrong-signature `equip X with Ordinal:` compiles clean** (latent validation gap). `builtin_traits` carries `("Ordinal", …)` with `int ordinal(self)` (`src/semantic/traits.rs:603`), but registration is gated on `scopes.lookup(name)` (`traits.rs:824`) and `Ordinal` is absent from the resolver's reservation list (`src/semantic/resolve.rs:127`) + no `lib/` declares `trait Ordinal` → the entry is dropped, the registry never holds an Ordinal `TraitInfo` → `equip X with Ordinal:` falls to the inherent-impl path (`trait_def_id=None`), signature NEVER validated (`@derive(Ordinal)` desugars to one, `derive.rs:87`). Repro: wrong-sig `equip Color with Ordinal:` → "OK"; same on `Comparable` is correctly rejected. Fix: add `"Ordinal"` to `resolve.rs:127` (cheapest), OR declare `trait Ordinal` in a lib. Both book (`appendix-traits.md:92`) + spec (`language-reference.md:~2705`/`~2783`) document it as real.

- **Compiler footguns (latent type-system validation gaps, same class as `Ordinal`):**
  - **(C3) Phantom static methods pass `gg check`, fail at C build/link.** `String.from("x")`/`int.from(5)`/`int.try_parse(s)` all report "OK" but `gg build` fails (implicit-decl / undefined-reference / phantom symbol). Root: `resolve_static_method_type` (`typecheck.rs:4717`, called `:1740`) returns `None` for an unknown `Type.method()` + the call FALLS THROUGH → the backend name-mangles into a nonexistent runtime symbol (rule-2 violation). Fix: emit a `NoSuchMethod`/`UnknownStaticMethod` diagnostic at typecheck. Canonical working: `String("x")`, `int.parse(s)`→Option, `parse_int(s)`→Result.
  - **`.unwrap()`/`.expect()`/`.unwrap_or()` on a NON-Option/Result is a silent no-op pass-through** (`methods.rs:588`, the `matches!(method_name, "unwrap"|"expect"|"unwrap_or")` arm returns the receiver unchanged). A genuine type confusion (e.g. `channel_exec(...).unwrap()` on a plain `CommandResult`) is silently swallowed. Fix (design call; verify no stdlib/self-host relies on the no-op first): make it a typecheck error or at minimum a lint.

- **Drop the imported-module typecheck-error truncate (writer-site fixes).** `check_items_recursive_tc` (`typecheck.rs`) truncates ALL errors from `Item::Module` to silence foreign-scope false positives. Fix (a) shipped (BinaryOp threads `decl_type_hint` for byte-literals). What's left blocking removal (a diagnostic run with truncate disabled surfaced these — none match the brief's (b)/(c)):
  - **Stale self-host code missing newer enum variants** at non-exhaustive `match` sites in `self_host_{parser,resolver,typechecker,lowerer,check}/{parser,resolve,format_gir,lower}.gg`: `EFString`/`ETry`/`EMove`/`EMutableBorrow`/`EAs`/`EAwait`/`ESpawn`/`ESpawnBlocking`/`ERethrow`/`ECatch` (Expr); `SMeta`/`SAssertReturn`/`SSnapshot`/`SMetaForMatch`/`SWith`/`SAssert`/`SNamedScope`/`SSelect`/`SOnError`/`SItem` (Stmt); `KwCatch`/`KwOn`/`KwSnapshot`; `GTNone`. Add catch-alls/handling across all 5 dirs.
  - **Real bug:** `no field 'span' found on type 'SpannedToken'` in `self_host_{parser,resolver}/parser.gg:2512`/`2485`.
  - **(b)/(c) NOT OBSERVED** — the self-host parser.gg has no `throws` functions; `eval.gg:1528`-style mismatches are in gorget-js code. Re-survey against current code before fixing.
  - Then: retire the stale self-host code + fix the SpannedToken.span access, THEN remove the truncate (`errors.truncate(error_count)` ~typecheck.rs:6071 + the `hard_errors` field + `hard_type_mismatch` call sites ~1364-1394). These aren't compiler gaps — fix the self-host code, don't perma-mask.

- **🆕 [LOW — self-host over-reject, DORMANT + UNREACHABLE; from the flip-tracks landing 2026-07-17] The self-host EStructLiteral single-owner-init arm lacks production's ref-typed-field skip** (`check_expr.rs:1183` `!target_is_ref` skips a `Ref[Callable]` field init — a borrow, not a copy). This arm rejects ALL args with no ref-field gate → would OVER-REJECT the exotic `S{cbref: f}` (cbref borrow-typed). Over-reject-only (never UAF); the arm is also structurally UNREACHABLE today (the parser never builds EStructLiteral — `S(...)` is ECall, `{...}` a dict/set). The per-field ref-flag mirror (`compute_struct_field_ref_flags`) is deferred. Note is at the EStructLiteral safety-walk arm in `typecheck.gg`.
- **🆕🐛 [MED — checker gap, from the 1C scout 2026-07-16] `Set[int] s; s[0]` PASSES `gg check` but FAILS C-compile** — Set has no index surface (`gorget_map_get` runs in set-mode sentinel); the checker should reject Set indexing outright (E_TypeMismatch family). Repro in `the CoW track-1C scout (git history)`. Own small slice; pairs with the coarse-kind-adjacent reject work.
- **🧹 [resources scout 2026-07-06, LOW] `to_string`/`String(int)` rejections need a FIX-IT** pointing at the canonical `f"{n}"` (and `.display()`). Today `E_NoMethodFound` is generic. Pairs with the A7 batch-4 ruling (reject-with-fix-it; f-strings outnumber alternatives ~97× in the corpus).

- **🐛 Local mutable-borrow binding is BROKEN + `&`-exclusivity UNENFORCED (owner-requested verification 2026-07-02; NOT urgent — reject eventually).** The spec makes `&` an EXCLUSIVE mutable borrow (`language-design.md:574-575` / `language-reference.md:2276-2277`: "any number of immutable borrows OR exactly one mutable borrow"; `reference §7.15` `:1747`: "the original ... cannot be accessed while the borrow is active"). The impl does not enforce it, and local `&`-bindings don't even produce a live borrow. Verified on `2194bd27` (`gg check`/`run`): `auto a = &b; b[1]=99; print(a[1])` → check OK, **runs to GARBAGE** (`auto a=&b` MISCOMPILES — Core #8 accept-and-miscompile); `Vector[int] &a = b; b[1]=99; print(a[1])` → check OK, prints the ORIGINAL `2` (`a` is a COPY, not a live alias). INTENDED (owner + spec): **REJECT** the code — `b` is inaccessible while mutably borrowed by `a` (borrow-exclusivity error); binding a mutable borrow to a local should either be that live-exclusive-borrow or be rejected as an unsupported form. Orthogonal to the full-lazy-CoW materialize track (this is borrow-check/lowering). When tackled: wire a `#[ignore]`d negative fixture (expected: reject) encoding the intent, then implement. Repros captured in session scratch.

- **🔵 TAIL-CALL OPTIMIZATION (TCO) — LATER (owner-set; a real language feature).** gg has no TCO. **Phase 1 (tractable, high-value): self-tail-recursion → loop** — detect a tail-position call to the SAME function (`return f(args)` returned DIRECTLY; ⚠ `return deep(n-1)+1` is NOT tail) THROUGH if/match/block tails, lower to param-rebind + jump-to-entry. Both compilers (Rust GIR/LIR + self-host `lower.gg`). **Phase 2 (defer): general/sibling tail calls** (`musttail`/trampoline). **Design Qs FIRST:** (a) GUARANTEED (Scheme-like, stack-safe — recommended given the safety pillar) vs best-effort; (b) ⚠ DROP/CoW ordering hazard — the tail jump REUSES the frame, so dead droppable locals must drop BEFORE the jump + a live droppable that outlives the call BLOCKS the TCO (UAF/leak otherwise); (c) the `assert return` postcondition runs AT the return. Non-tail deep recursion (`stack_guard_deep_recursion`) intentionally NOT covered (owner chose honest OS-default-stack). Gate: a tail-recursive fixture runs at 1e7+ depth on an 8MB stack; `fixed_point` GREEN; no ASan drop/leak regression.

- **🆕 `void ?? int` invariant-#8 reject-in-both candidate (from async_rwlock scout):** Rust gg ACCEPTS a genuine `void ?? int` (null-coalesce with a void/unit LHS) — only a cc warning, then runs it. A `??` on a void LHS is ill-formed; both compilers should REJECT it. Bounded single-node rejection (opportunistic, per the Phase-1 steer). Unrelated to async_rwlock (which has no `??`).
- **🆕 [MEDIUM — same staleness family as R-A's Fix 1, from R-A pass-1 review 2026-07-10] `ast_type_to_resolved` binds stale Import placeholders in TYPE positions.** It uses value-first `lookup` (`src/semantic/types.rs:403`) and ACCEPTS `DefKind::Import` (`:413`) — so a cross-module imported trait in a type position binds the stale placeholder DefId, and e.g. `Box[ImportedTrait]`'s trait-object detection (`:424-433`, requires `kind == DefKind::Trait` at `:428`) silently fails cross-module. Same import-placeholder-staleness family as the R-A trait-registry keying fix, but a DIFFERENT read path — needs its own scout (what breaks: trait objects? equip resolution? generic bounds?) + fixtures. Own track.
### Low

- **[LOW — A2-R2 M2 polish 2026-07-12] `E_MoveWithoutOperator` field/index sub-place message renders the ROOT name, not the exact sub-place.** `tainted_place_name` (`src/semantic/safety/helpers.rs`) uses `find_root_def_id`, which returns only the ROOT binding's name — so a field/index sub-place (`hh.r`, `v[0]`) renders `hh.clone()` / `v.clone()` instead of `hh.r.clone()` / `v[0].clone()`. The A2-R2 M2 message sidesteps the inaccuracy ("copy the sub-place with `hh.clone()` … a bare `!` on a field/index sub-place is a partial move"), but the exact sub-place text is nicer. Fix = thread the place SPAN through `tainted_place_name` and slice the source (or have a place-printer walk the identifier/self/field/tuple/index chain). Not required by the capture-no-`!` GATE. Own scout.
- **✨ FEATURE — support forward / cross-module const-refs in a `const` initializer (fixpoint registration; Low)** (filed by the const-foldability round, 2026-06-25, DONE `316d818a`). A `const A = B + 1` where `B` is a const declared LATER (or imported, or a meta-const referenced from a `meta if` elif/else arm — the Track-9 re-review confirmed these ALL fold to 0 pre-fix, a pre-existing lowering gap now surfaced consistently at check time) currently REJECTS ("not a compile-time constant") because the lowering const-scan (`src/ir/lowering/mod.rs:546`) AND the matching typecheck pre-pass (`check_module_const_foldability`) fold in a SINGLE source-order pass — `B` isn't in `module_constants` when `A` is folded. Rejecting is correct-for-now (it used to silently miscompile to 0; reject is strictly better + the typecheck/lowering agree). To SUPPORT it: make the const-fold loop a FIXPOINT (iterate until no new const folds) in BOTH the lowering scan and the typecheck pre-pass (keep them mirrored — one source of truth). Then `const_forward_ref_error.gg` flips from reject to `11`; update it + its test. Pure ergonomics; no correctness gap today.

- **🌀 DEFERRED: lexical `wrapping { … }` scope** (filed when `--overflow` was retired 2026-06-21). After the flag retirement, `+%`/`-%`/`*%` (per-op) are the SOLE wrapping escape. A lexical `wrapping{}` block (scoped wrapping for a hot loop body, mirroring how `catch` is lexical) would be the ergonomic analogue — it lowers to the same `AddWrap`/`SubWrap`/`MulWrap` GIR ops (additive, no backend work). **NOT needed now:** `+%` suffices and the self-host self-compile runs fully checked and isn't overflow-bound (proof always-checked is acceptable). Add ONLY if measurement ever demands it; the self-host compile is the benchmark. Own small scout→brief→reviews if funded.

- **🐛 `gg check` PERMISSIVENESS (follow-up to `9e7ba9f4`): `gg check` ACCEPTS a bare `return` inside `int throws E` (any non-void `T throws E`)** → silently lowers to `Ok(0)`; arguably a "missing return value" error. Low-pri symmetry; lowering is now well-defined.

- **(Low/Medium, CORRECTNESS) Static-decl initializers are NOT type-checked against their declared type.** `public static int X = [1,2,3]` is accepted by `gg check` while the LOCAL form is rejected. Mostly latent, BUT Bug-B Stage 1 changed its symptom for collection-literal RHS (`initializer_needs_synthetic_fn` keys on RHS shape → garbage pointer + C warning). Real fix = add static-decl initializer type-checking (mirror the local var-decl check).

- **`@[no_alloc]` function annotation**: compiler error on allocating operations.

- **🆕 [LOW-MED — production OVER-REJECTION (false positive), from RV-D pass-1 2026-07-16] Production's match fall-through suppression keys ONLY on a separate `else_arm` (`check_stmt.rs:1217-1222`) — a bare unguarded `case _:`/`case x:` wildcard arm does NOT suppress it, so "moved-before + reinit in ALL arms incl. wildcard + use-after" is falsely REJECTED** (the arm is genuinely exhaustive). The RV-D-patched self-host is strictly more precise here (sound). Fix production to treat unguarded wildcard/binding arms as exhaustive (mirror the self-host's `match_has_catch_all`); dormant, no corpus hits. Pairs with the may-move §9.5 prose.
- **🧹 [coarse-kind Fable output-review 2026-07-16, LOW — diagnostic-cascade polish] Production emits a cascading second `E_AwaitNonFuture` on the double-await shape** (`await await g()`) where the self-host emits only the primary `E_DoubleAwait`. Both lanes reject; both diagnostics are true; purely a cascade-suppression nicety. Fixture `double_await_error.gg` is the probe.
## Backend / codegen

### High

- **🆕📐 [OWNER DIRECTIVE 2026-07-13 — standing] RETIRE sidecars + parallel structures (audit sweep).** "All opportunities to reduce sidecars and parallel structures should be taken." Upgrades layering rule 3 to a HUNT-AND-FOLD mandate (decisions.md standing directives + the CallArg LOG entry). AUDIT both compilers (Rust `src/` + self-host `.gg`) for index-aligned parallel vectors / hand-synced side-tables splitting a single logical fact — normalize into cohesive records/typed fields. The `CallArg{name,ownership,value}` normalization is the exemplar (retires the parallel names-vector). Known/suspected fossils: self-host `StructRegistry` parallel `Vector[String]`+`Vector[int]` scan; Dict-state-loss parallel-storage; `type_info_keys_safe` wrapper; assess `resolution_map`/side-tables (legitimate vs foldable). Deliverable = ranked list (impact × risk) + fold recs; each fold its own gauntlet. Read-only scout first. **Do NOT conflate with systematic data-oriented SoA (the ECS track below) — that's the disciplined form, not this smell.**
- **🐛 [BACKEND DIVERGENCE — resources decision scout 2026-07-06] LLVM does not honor `alloc=` into a bare Arena the way C does.** The bare-allocator leak (q2a2) and the `.destroy()`-then-use UAF (q2b2) reproduce on C ASan but NOT on LLVM — LLVM appears not to route `Vector/String(alloc=a)` into the arena at all. Backends must be at parity (whichever semantics the A10 ruling picks). Probes: /tmp/recover_scout_resources/.



- **🆕🐛 [HIGH — MERGED 2026-07-21 (M-infer): ONE fix, THREE symptoms] `infer_collection_element_type` decides collection element types from mangled NAME PREFIXES instead of a typed field.** Root/fix (shared): `methods.rs:3862` — carry K/V TypeIds on the collection TypeDef (fixes read + write at once), not re-parse the mangled name. All THREE distinct silent-wrong-output symptoms are retained verbatim:
  - **[LOW — pre-existing, shared read+write; from R36-C] `infer_collection_element_type` compound-Dict-key first-`__` heuristic** (`methods.rs:~3773` + self-host `index_value_type_name`). Dict K/V split on the FIRST `__` in `Dict__K__V` is ambiguous for a COMPOUND key (`Dict[Vector[int], Option[int]]`). Pre-existing + shared (the `d[k]` index-read path has the identical heuristic), so R36-C's put/get_or_put hint is CONSISTENT, not a new regression. Real fix = carry K/V TypeIds on the Dict TypeDef instead of re-parsing the mangled name (fixes read + write at once).
  - **(rule-2 debt) `infer_collection_element_type` (`src/ir/lowering/exprs/methods.rs`) decides element types from NAME PREFIXES** (`Vector__`/`Dict__`/`Map__`). Element-type metadata should be a typed field on the collection TypeDef (`element_type` axis set at `register_collection_alias`/`ensure_collection_type`), read via an accessor. The String-base comprehension no longer reaches it; every other caller still keys on the prefix (a new collection family falls to I64). Fix the axis, not the next caller.
  - **🆕🐛 [HIGH — SILENT WRONG OUTPUT today, from the 1C brief-review pass-1 2026-07-16] Rust `HashMap`-of-struct ELEMENT TYPING is broken: `infer_collection_element_type` (`methods.rs:3859`) strips `Dict__`/`Map__` but NOT `HashMap__` → `HashMap__int64_t__Point` resolves to I64 (fallback `:3871`).** Measured: `HashMap[int,Point]` field READ `d[0].x` prints `0` (pre-existing, no 1C involvement); whole-element read `Point p = d[0]` C-COMPILE-FAILS; scalar `HashMap[int,int]` works. ⚠ ASYMMETRY: the self-host twin `index_value_type_name` (`lower_types.gg:2648`) DOES handle `HashMap__` — Rust is less complete than its own mirror. The 1C write-gate was SCOPED to `Array|OrderedMap` because of this (the Map arm would silently route HashMap field-writes into wrong output); **this track's fix also flips the 1C gate arm to include `Map` + adds the HashMap shape to the 1C fixture** (one-line flip once the typing is fixed — verify construction/put end-to-end first, the cc-fail suggests the mis-typing is wider than reads). **⚠ ASYMMETRY NOTE (1C pass-3 2026-07-16): post-1C the SELF-HOST already writes through HashMap-of-struct fields** (it has no CkHashMap — `resource_meta_for` maps `HashMap__`→`CkDict`, `lir_lower.gg:318`, and `index_value_type_name` resolves HashMap values, `lower_types.gg:2648`) **while scoped Rust prints 0 — the HashMap read/store is ITSELF broken by the element-typing bug (methods.rs:3859), independent of the write-through gate (a store-then-read with NO field write also prints 0; 1C pass-5 measured) — a LATENT Rust≠self-host divergence (zero corpus sites today). This track closes it by fixing RUST (strip `HashMap__` at methods.rs:3859 + admit `Map` in the 1C gate), restoring parity.** Own scout→brief→gauntlet.
- **🆕🐛 [MEDIUM — codegen type-precision; discovered 2026-07-24 chasing the CI timeout] Self-host emits `int32 → void*` assignments that Apple clang 21 rejects (hard error, un-`-w`-suppressible).** The self-host's `--lir-c` output contains `void* __s2 = __v2;` where `__v2 = (int32_t)0LL` — a NULL-ish sentinel the lowering typed as `int32` but stored into a `void*` slot (repro line: stage1.c:~22866, `__s2 = __v2`). Functionally 0 (benign at runtime) but type-incorrect C: gcc / older clang emit a `-Wint-conversion` *warning* (suppressed by the `cc -w` in the self-host stage tests), so **CI (Linux/gcc) is unaffected**; Apple clang 21 promotes `-Wint-conversion` to a default *error* that `-w` does not downgrade. Consequence: `self_host_stage1_clone_ceiling` / `self_host_bootstrap_fixed_point` / the pinned-8MB self-compile **cannot run locally on recent macOS** (cc step fails at ~135s, well before any assert). Fix = give the sentinel/slot a consistent pointer type at the lowering source (the void* slot should receive `(void*)0`, not an int32), NOT paper over with `-Wno-int-conversion`. Pre-existing (codegen; predates the 2026-07-24 timeout fix). Verify it still repros before fixing.

- **🐛 RUST-GG CODEGEN — `rethrow (String e): <String-transform>` LEAKS the transformed error String (LeakSanitizer)** (DISCOVERED 2026-06-22 by the throws-codegen-fix output-review's ASan sweep; PRE-EXISTING — reproduces IDENTICALLY on the pristine `717289b4` tree, NOT introduced by the throws-codegen fix). The freshly-formatted/allocated transform String (the new error payload built in the rethrow arm) is never freed on the error path. ⚠ ONLY the `rethrow`-with-a-String-transform shape leaks — `rethrow_basic`/`rethrow_bare` (no String-build transform) are CLEAN, and the entire 68-fixture throws/error ASan sweep is otherwise leak-free. Own scout (RUN under ASan, trace the unfreed alloc — `gorget_string_format` at the rethrow arm) → brief → fix at the rethrow-transform OWNERSHIP-register write site (register the transform-result String for drop / move it into the propagated Error). Low-pri (leak, not UB), but it is a real defect on BOTH the reference and self-host shape. NOT a quick guess.

Extend the `reason` field to `CallExtern` (or a companion sidecar) and route the closure clone through a tagged emitter so the census covers it.

- **Rust frontend: unify the 7 `lower_for_*` functions into a single scaffold + per-type element extractor** (`src/ir/lowering/stmts/for_loops.rs`, 1159 lines, ~80% duplicated scaffold). Self-host's `lower_loops.gg::lower_for` (`lower_for_vector` fast path + `lower_for_iterator` protocol) is the reference-grade shape. **Three layers** (ship in order, green tests gating each): (1) helper extraction (`alloc_for_blocks`/`emit_else_arm_tail`/`init_borrow_iter_local`, ~150 lines retired, no semantic change, ~half day); (2) filter abstraction for dict/set (`with_state_filter`, ~30 lines, ~a day total); (3) trait-based unification (`trait CollectionLowerer` + `lower_for_scaffold<L>` + 7 backends, ~300-400 lines, highest win + highest risk, 2-3 days total). Per-kind variability that doesn't unify: `lower_for_string` (source-aware ptr setup), `lower_for_range` (no iter_op), dict/set (state-check filter + output-param destructure), `lower_for_iterable` (no incr_bb). Verification: each layer ships independently with a full integration sweep (1122/1122). Tied-off after: the trait-equipped-methods-in-`fn_sigs` entry + the iter-chain `lowerer_comparison` mismatches.

- **Runtime-side panic locations (~50+ sites in `src/backend/c/c_runtime.rs`).** Compiler-side emit produces `file:line:col:` for inline traps, but runtime helpers (`gorget_array_index_oob`, `gorget_str_*_oob`, channel-closed sends, alloc failures, `gorget_panic("integer overflow")` at `c_runtime.rs:5042/5047/5052`, +~45) are called from inside other runtime fns with no caller span → fall back to `<unknown>:0:0:`. Proper fix = per-runtime-function span plumbing (every panic-able entry point grows `(const char* file, int line, int col)`, every emit site passes the LIR instruction's span). Estimate 5-8 days.

- **Residual: `Option[Box[T]]` / `Result[Box[T]]` field drops not emitted on enum variants and struct fields.** The Box-field-drop wrapper + struct/enum-variant wiring closed 2026-05-01. The Option/Result ENUM-VARIANT skip (`populate_recursive_drop_enums` mod.rs:471-481) + STRUCT-FIELD skip (`populate_recursive_drop_structs` mod.rs:412-422) kept intentional: enabling it crashes the self-host `resolve_stmt` because `stmts.get(i).unwrap()` returns `Stmt` by value (shallow copy aliasing the vector's interior box/string pointers) → double-free. Proper fix is COMPILER-level: make `Vector[T].get(i)` for resource T auto-clone (deep) or return `Ref[T]`-only. Then the skip can be removed + `option_box_enum.gg`'s 3 leaked Some(Box(...)) free correctly. Today: leak (3 blocks), not unsoundness.

- **Stdlib narrow waist — Phase 2c residual.** `Vector.each`/`for_each` void-return entries retired (user-space `lib/std/iter.gg` wrappers). Typed-return Vector HOFs (filter/map/fold/reduce/any/all/find/find_index/count/enumerate/flat_map/zip) STAY as BuiltinMethodDecl entries — IR-lowering reads their declared return types via `resolve_builtin_method_return_type` when the user-space wrapper sig isn't registered yet (early generic mono). Full retirement blocks on a separate signature source for IR-lowering when BuiltinMethodDecl is absent. Dict.each/Set.each BuiltinMethodDecls stay (no wrapper migration). LIR `HofOp` variants stay (serve Dict/Set too).

- **C backend: retire local val_types/ptr_pointee fixup phases (after the 2026-05-15 seed migration).** Seeds `val_types`/`ptr_pointee` from `func.value_types`/`func.pointee_types`; remaining cross-instruction reasoning to push upstream so the local pass disappears: Guard accessor inference from consumers (`mod.rs:1381-1456`), cross-type Option/Result map combinator override (`mod.rs:1465-1486` via `combinator_result_struct_id`), consumer-driven Add/Cmp peer-type back-propagation (`mod.rs:1490-1570`), InlineC→SlotStore type inference (`mod.rs:1306-1322`), Ret-from-function backfill. Each needs `compute_module_value_types` to gain a fixed-point/multi-phase walk. Half-day each + tests.

- **Decompose emit_call_extern.rs (~908 lines).** Tier 1-3 + HOF (→`emit_hof.rs`) + printf (→`emit_printf.rs`) lifted. Remaining genuinely-backend-specific clusters: out-parameter adaptation (~178 lines), collection drop/clone injection (~70 lines).

- **Phase A's `resources` build-tooling — the cross-language single source of truth** (`docs/devbook/18-runtime-abi.md` + `26-self-host-frontend.md`). Canonical source = idiomatic Gorget code at `compiler/data/{schema,resources}.gg`, baked via `include_str!` + parsed by the compiler's own parser (no build.rs/codegen; self-host imports the same files). Items 1-6 done (runtime_extern_sig retirement, CollectionKind close, spike, Rust loader + AST walker, self-host adoption, self-host `starts_with` consumer-read retirement). Remaining:
  - **Item 7 — retire Rust name-prefix dispatch sites.** PARTIAL (first wave: `ResourceKind::RefCounted` + 3 sites migrated to `resources::table().lookup(name)` reading typed `collection_kind`). Site survey: `starts_with("[A-Z][a-zA-Z]*__"` = 256 hits (TODO's old "9" was the headline). Sub-items (7e is load-bearing — 7b + most 7d retire once it lands):
    - **7b. `emit_collection_constructor`** (`helpers.rs:824-915`) — typed Tier 1 landed (7e Phase 4 `4aa0cdcb`); Tier 2 name-parse fallback retires once 7e-r1 makes Resource operands universal.
    - **7d. residual `starts_with` sites** (`emit_types.rs`, `validate.rs`, `lifts.rs`, `mod.rs`) — mostly WRITER stages reading element/key/value types from mangled names before val_types is computed. Bundled under 7e-r1 + 7e-r3.
    - **7e. LIR element-type parametricity — the writer-site fix that retires name-parsing as a CLASS.** Encoding done (`LirType::Resource { kind, params }` SSoT + `expected_resource_arity`/`validate_resource_arity`; `emit_collection_constructor` prefers typed reads). Residue (all gated on extending the writer-side `Resource` propagation beyond `Inst::CollectionCtor` results): **7e-r1 global writer flip** (`map_gir_type_with_structs` returns `Resource{kind,params}` for every resource-type GIR Named operand; ~64 c_lir `LirType::Struct(sid)` sites need parallel `Resource` arms; helpers `parametric_resource_lir_type`+`classify_resource_name` recoverable from `2a337b6a..490a45b2`); **7e-r3 Phase 5 fallback retirement** (once r1 universal, delete `emit_collection_constructor` Tier 2 + `elem_size_from_monomorphized`/`dict_elem_sizes_from_monomorphized`/`concurrency_elem_size` name-reads in `types.rs`). Encoding (a) `Resource{kind, params:SmallVec<[LirType;2]>}` is the conceptually-clean path (perf to be benchmarked, not assumed); (b) per-mono StructId is the alternative. Spike (a) for half a day + measure compile-time on the lowerer driver before deciding. Estimate 3-5 days, design-attention, schedule deliberately.

- **🆕🐛 [MED — generic-indirection escape, from the RV-A Fable review 2026-07-16] `Box[T].x` / `Guard[T].x` through a generic fn (`T getx[T](Box[T] b): return b.x`) checks OK and prints garbage-0 — pre-existing at base, reproduced both binaries.** The Box shape is the generic-param variant of the deref-backend gap (rides that entry); **the Guard[T] case is its own line**: direct guard field access WORKS, generic-indirected prints 0 — a monomorphization-side hole in the guards-work premise. No fixture blesses either. Own slice with the deref-backend track.

- **🆕🐛 [MED — parser disambiguator, from the D29 scout 2026-07-16] `fs[i](10)` where `fs: Vector[int(int)]` fails `error[E_NotAFunction]`** — the `[...]` postfix disambiguator (`src/parser/expr.rs:1257`) greedily parses `fs[i](...)` as a generic call. Pre-existing, independent of D29 (adjacent to the `v[i]()!` corner). Workaround: bind to a local. Own small slice.

- **🐛 [unwrap fix scout 2026-07-06, MEDIUM] `expect("custom msg")`'s message argument is DROPPED at lowering** — `methods.rs:975` lowers `expect` to `__option_unwrap` ignoring its args, so expect prints the generic unwrap message (post-tag-check-fix it at least TRAPS). Fix: thread the message operand through to the panic emit. May fold into the unwrap track if cheap; else own small track.

- **🧹 [unwrap fix scout 2026-07-06, LOW] Two fossils + a wrong doc:** (a) the `unwrap_error` `abort()` combinator paths in `emit_hof.rs:156`/`emit_types.rs`/`llvm/mod.rs` are DEAD for user code (the LIR Tier-2a path intercepts first) — retire when touched; (b) `EnumKind::Option`'s doc comment in `src/lir/mod.rs` says "Tag 0 = None" — WRONG, the real convention is tag 0 = Some/Ok (per emitted C, `unwrap_or` :4064, `is_some` :4198) — fix the comment before it misleads someone into a real bug.

- **🐛 [strmove fix scout 2026-07-06, MEDIUM — ASan-verified, ORTHOGONAL to the concat fix] `String &p` (mut-borrow) param grown by `.push()` LEAKS the grown buffer.** `void f(String &p): p.push("!!!"); print(p)` → LSan 6 bytes. String-specific — `Vector &v` push through the same shared-mutation Borrowed path is clean. This same leaky path is WHY the concat fix deliberately kept `!`-move String params `Owned` (routing them through Borrowed would inherit this leak — measured). Fix the Borrowed-String grow path; the concat track does NOT depend on it. Evidence: /tmp/recover_strmove/FINDINGS.md.

- **🐛 LLVM backend: `--sanitize` is SILENTLY DROPPED — an "LLVM ASan" run is VACUOUS.** `compile_llvm_pipeline` (`src/main.rs:1177`) never reads `options.sanitize`; the LLVM clang/link path passes nothing (binary has zero asan symbols). Any LLVM-side memory gate must use STDOUT PARITY with the C backend. Fix: at minimum warn loudly when `--sanitize` + `--backend=llvm`; properly pass `-fsanitize=address,undefined` through the LLVM link step.

- **🐛 LLVM backend: in-loop sret/temp allocas emitted PER ITERATION → a print-heavy loop stack-exhausts (SIGSEGV) at ~399k bool-prints** (pre-existing). Each iteration's `alloca %GorgetString` is at its use site inside the loop → frame grows with trip count (C backend has block-scoped autos, no cliff). Fix = hoist allocas to the function entry block (also defeats mem2reg otherwise). Until then keep LLVM loop probes under ~100k prints.

- **🐛 ASan: residual Rust-`gg` leak cohort AFTER the print-temp leak-class fix** (re-measure via `gg build --sanitize` + `ASAN_OPTIONS=detect_leaks=1`): `cli_basic` 7B/1 (`strip_dashes` result), `yaml_parse` 507B/138 (method-body escape-clones), `string_conversions` 94B/2 (`std.conv` extern results), `datetime_format` 46B/4 (extern results), `fstring_method_chain` 16B/2 (closure-env). NOT the print-temp class — each an other-class drop-registration gap. RUN an ASan witness per fixture, trace the unregistered materialization, register at the producer.

- **🐛 ASan: `print_trait_object` 61B/2 — trait-dispatch `display` result never drop-registered.** The print-temp fix registers the `Type__display` result at `format_for_printf`'s Displayable branch, but a `display` through a TRAIT OBJECT (Box+vtable) routes through trait-call lowering, which never registers the returned owned String. Register the call result at the producer in trait-dispatch lowering. (Box-related residue may be a second gap.)

- **🐛 Print-temp site-C ungated sub-shape: a Clone-of-NON-string-AGGREGATE f-string interp temp stays UNREGISTERED** (deliberate gate in `lower_interp_segment` branches 2/3, `calls.rs`). Registered only under Move or Clone-of-String, because `AssignMode::Clone` on a non-string aggregate is a SHALLOW memcpy today (registering the alias would double-free). If such an aggregate owns heap, it leaks. Unblocks when Clone deep-copies aggregates — then drop the `is_string_type` half at both branches.

- **🐛 ASan: `gorget_js_snag_3_match_struct_literal_use` stack-buffer-overflow in `__interceptor_memcpy`** (pre-existing, IDENTICAL in baseline). A match-struct-literal miscompile (a struct-literal in a match arm over-reads on memcpy). RUN-repro under ASan, GIR/LIR-dump the match-struct-literal lowering, find the size mismatch (likely a payload-size vs slot-size disagreement at the match-arm bind).

- **🐛 LATENT DCE gap: `has_side_effects` (`src/lir/optimize.rs:576`) omits `Inst::CallRuntime`** (lists `CallExtern`/`Call`/`CallPtr`/`CallClosure` but NOT `CallRuntime`). A value-returning `CallRuntime` with an UNUSED dst AND real side effects would be wrongly DCE-eliminated. VOID `CallRuntime`s are SAFE (`inst.dst()` is None → kept). Fix = add `Inst::CallRuntime { .. }` to the `matches!` set. Verify no perf regression.

- **Remove name-matching from consuming-position lowering — add an EXPLICIT per-method consuming-param signal (hardened by reviewer passes 1+2).** `lower_method_call` (`src/ir/lowering/exprs/methods.rs:1850-1861`, `consuming_positions_by_name`) identifies consuming arg positions by method NAME (push/add/extend/send/push_back/push_front→0; put/set/insert→0,1) + `is_string_builder_method` exclusion. Feeds FOUR name-matched consumers: `pre_call_clone_temps` (~:1908), `move_zero_locals` (~:2143), `consuming_clone_temps` (~:2235), `value_arg_idx_for_method` (:1822-1825). A 2026-05-25 band-aid (`07649296`) added a `ParamABI::ByPtr` filter (closed the leak, left the name match). **Current state is CORRECT** — this is a cleanliness refactor (+ closes pre-existing `get_or_put`/`fill`/`extend` gaps), NOT a bug fix.
  - **PROPER fix: add `consumes_params: &'static [usize]` to `BuiltinMethodDecl`** (arg positions whose ownership transfers), set per-method ONLY for true transfers: array push[1]/insert[2]/set[2]/extend[1]/fill[2], map put key+value[1,2], set add[1]/insert[1], heap push[1], channel send[1], guard/write_guard set[1]. EMPTY for `update` (clones), `sort*` (closure), `contains`/`get`/`remove` (read-only).
  - **PASS-1's `is_mutating` trigger is UNSOUND — confirmed false-positive LEAKS** (`Dict.update` `is_mutating:true` but `gorget_map_update`→`gorget_map_put_cloned` CLONES; `Vector.sort*` `is_mutating:true` but no consumed element). `is_mutating` ≠ ownership-transfer. The by-void-ptr table `collection_void_param_indices` (`helpers.rs:681`) is a SUPERSET incl. read-only `contains`/`get`/`remove` — cross-check for position ACCURACY only, don't copy wholesale.
  - **INDEXING (off-by-one):** `consumes_params` is self-INCLUSIVE 1-based (matches `move_indices`/`fn_param_ownerships[i+1]`); the sibling `params` field is self-EXCLUSIVE. State in the field doc.
  - **CHURN:** `BuiltinMethodDecl` has 180 `&'static` literals + `params`/`return_type` are fn-pointers (`#[derive(Default)]` IMPOSSIBLE) → land a declarative builder-macro / hand-`impl Default` FIRST, then set `consumes_params` on ~11 entries.
  - **Then:** (1) drive `register_collection_method_sigs` ownership off this field; (2) derive `consuming_positions` from `ownership==Move`, delete all 4 name-matched sites + `is_string_builder_method` + the `ByPtr` filter. For Dict source key/value `TypeId`s from `GenericCollector::instances_raw()` (`generics/mod.rs:2225`), NOT mangled-name splitting.
  - **Non-collection builtins (Channel/Guard/WriteGuard) need their OWN registration path** (verified they never reach `register_collection_method_sigs` — `scan_type` `generics/mod.rs:~390` registers only Vector/Dict/HashMap/Set/HashSet/Box). EITHER add these bases to the `scan_type` allowlist OR give the new path its own mangled-instance source. Read `consumes_params` via `lookup_protocol(base)`/`protocol_for_mangled_name` (`builtins.rs:~989`). Register `fn_param_ownerships`(+`fn_param_abis`) ONLY — NOT `fn_sigs` (return-type-resolution precedence at `methods.rs:~2051`); `populate_consume_externs` (`mod.rs:1860`) picks up the new Move entries automatically.
  - **⚠ Blast radius:** `consume_externs` feeds the FATAL Tier-2a validator (`mod.rs:~1870`) — a false-positive `Move` becomes a COMPILE error (fails loud, but test for it).
  - **Gates (the obvious ones are BLIND; existing leak fixtures use only PRIMITIVE int):** add resource-`T` leak-probe fixtures for Dict put/set, **`Channel[String].send`** (highest-risk), Guard/WriteGuard `[String].set`, Vector extend/fill, Set insert; AND a `Dict.update(other_with_resource_values)` probe asserting NO leak; AND a `Dict.get_or_put` MISS-path probe with a resource-typed default (the default is both `gorget_map_put`'d AND SlotStored on miss — characterize, don't fix here). Deque: protocol-table only (not constructable). Revert if it regresses.

The hardcoded `call_extern("gorget_panic", …)` at `src/ir/lowering/stmts/mod.rs:2132` for `assert` remains. #### [CLASS FIX — layering, from Increment-2 scout + Round-8 Track A] typed is_borrow_ptr flag on GirFieldInfo
Multiple sites reconstruct "is this field a borrow pointer" from the field-type-NAME prefix `Ref__`/`MutRef__` (core-invariant-#2 smell): `lir_lower.gg:4360` GIFieldLoad dispatch (Round-8 Track A added the `MutRef__` read), `resolve_field_lir_type:708`, `resolve_field_gir_type:942`, and now closure Increment-2's ByMutRef env field (which must be NAMED `MutRef__<T>` to take the load-stored-pointer path). All reuse the documented accepted residual because `GirFieldInfo` stores only a type-name String, not a typed id. CLEAN CLASS FIX: add a typed `bool is_borrow_ptr` (or a typed pointee-id) to `GirFieldInfo` (gir.gg), set it at field registration, and read it at all the above sites instead of the name prefix — retiring the whole residual class. Mirrors Rust's typed `field_is_ptr` (insts.rs:1029). Medium track (gir.gg + ~4 consumers + careful review); do AFTER the closure increments that depend on the current residual behavior have landed (so the cleanup is a pure no-op refactor). This subsumes the earlier "MutRef__ symmetry" nit.

- **🆕 [MEDIUM — typed-metadata smell, from the R-C scout 2026-07-10] The LIR extern decl hardcodes an `i64` return for the monomorphized `Result__T__E__unwrap_error` mangles** — the combinator route's return type should come from typed metadata (the Result's error-payload type), not a default; the R-C guard works around it at the LLVM emit (val_types dst = field-2 payload) and the C twin has its own resolution. Root fix = type the extern decl at the LIR producer (rule 2), retiring both backend-side resolutions. Pairs with the `Inst::CallExtern` typed-routing-flag cleanup in the review-residuals (f) bundle.
### Low

- **🔭 (low-pri, const-bytes #10) — scalar-FLOAT static const-init uses `%g` (`float_to_str`), not bit-exact.** `scalar_static_c_literal`'s float path round-trips `2.5` exactly, but a future precision-sensitive float static (`3.14159265358979`) would `%g`-truncate. Switch to a `.17e`/LE-byte encoding when such a static appears.

### High

- **[HIGH — PROVISIONAL re-pin, do not forget — lag-close Wave 2 2026-07-21] Stage-0/1 clone ceilings raised; charter is hand-optimal clone count.** Measured + provisional re-pin `fe83cf25` (measured + ~1%): stage-0 array **12,766,424** (+0.37% over old ceiling) · string **30,385,755** (+3.4%) · stage-1 array **1,034,938,322** · string **1,926,672,347**. **Attribution run on tip (2026-07-21, `scripts/clone_attribution.sh` release gg → driver self-compile):** aggregates match (`array_clone=12766424` `string_clone=30385755`); top instrumented hits are still the **#13 return-view / bind-return class** (`ReturnFromBorrow`+`VarDeclFromBorrow`; reclaim = return-view lazy materialization, **not shipped**), not Wave 2 `+=`: #1 `ReturnFromBorrow` `SpannedToken`/`parser.gg:1018` **7.42M**; then `VarDeclFromBorrow` `Vector[int]`/`lir_ssa.gg:500` 1.58M; string `ReturnFromBorrow`/`VarDeclFromBorrow` in lir_codegen/lir_lower. **By-reason rollup:** `ReturnFromBorrow` 11.0M + `VarDeclFromBorrow` 10.8M ≈ **71%** of 30.7M site hits; `CoWMaterialization` only 1.19M. **Caveat:** instrument covers ~top-level events only (~3.5% of leaf volume historically). **Reviewer suspicion (SH `v += w` clone+extend as string_clone source) is NOT supported for the self-compile workload** — no top site is Array compound/concat; self-host source barely uses `Vector +=`. Ceiling bump is more likely **more SH frontend surface / more bind-return clones**, not a new concat path in the compiler-under-test. **Still provisional** — no tip-vs-pre-Wave2 A/B yet. **Opt pass:** re-measure → tip-vs-base attribution A/B → reclaim via #13 return-view lazy materialization / DEEP-1 → re-seed DOWN. Separate (correctness, not ceiling): `Vector[String] +/+=` shallow-extend double-free is fixed in `gorget_array_extend` (elem_clone on new range) — see DONE.md.
