- **[MED — scout] Shared self-compile artifacts across the heavy self-host tests.** ~7 full driver self-compiles today (`self_host_bootstrap` + `bootstrap_fixed_point` [plain stage chain]; `self_host_clone_ceiling` [instrumented stage-0]; `self_host_stage1_clone_ceiling` [instrumented stage-1]; `runtime_diff`+`lowerer_comparison`+`c_emit_comparison` [self-host corpus compile]) each rebuild their stage(s) independently — the biggest slice of the ~52-min debug sweep tail. Collapse to ~4 DISTINCT artifacts each computed ONCE, fanning the assertions out. NOT "one compile total": `bootstrap_fixed_point` irreducibly needs a multi-stage chain (a fixed point is proven by consecutive-stage byte-identity), and the ceiling tests need the instrumented (`--clones`) binary, not the plain one. The scout's real problem is test ISOLATION under cargo's parallel model: a memoized read-only fixture (`Once`) vs merging related assertions into one test. Owner-accepted 2026-07-20; keep every assertion — this is dedup, not a coverage cut. Serves self-host iteration speed (north-star-adjacent).
- **[MED — scout] `--clones=stats` unsupported under `--backend=llvm` → the LLVM lane's clone behavior is UNMEASURED by the ceilings (owner-filed 2026-07-20).** `gg build --clones=stats --backend=llvm` errors and exits (`src/main.rs:2703`). Root cause (from the code's own note): under LLVM's linking/interposition model the `__gorget_clone_site_hit` counter table would be sized for the wrong module's `CloneId` range → silent misattribution; the C path dodges this by keeping the hit fn `static`. Consequence: the clone-ceiling de-opt detectors (`self_host_clone_ceiling`, `self_host_stage1_clone_ceiling`) run on the C backend ONLY, so a clone-bomb reachable via LLVM-specific lowering would slip them — a real parity blind spot given backends are meant to be at parity. Compile-time modes (`sites`/`verbose`/`sites-tsv`) already work on LLVM (pre-backend). Fix: per-module `static` counter table under LLVM (mirror the C interposition-safe shape), or a module-scoped CloneId base offset so the table sizes correctly. NOT a runtime-workaround concern (opt-in instrument, off by default in emitted programs). Design the counter-table lifetime from `src/backend/llvm/` module boundaries; cite the C shape in `runtime_clone_sites.c`.
  - **[folded 2026-07-21 from the 2026-07-02 duplicate copy — impl steps, UNION]** **🧱 `--clones=stats` under `--backend=llvm` is REJECTED, not supported (filed by DEEP-1 slice 0, 2026-07-02).** The reject (clean error, was a silent no-op — even the aggregate `[clone-stats]` line never printed) lives at the `TODO(llvm-clone-stats)` site in `src/main.rs` (grep it). Real support path, per the comment there: (a) append `RUNTIME_CLONE_STATS` + `render_clone_sites_runtime(clone_site_count)` to the hand-composed LLVM runtime blob in `compile_llvm_pipeline` (`src/main.rs`), (b) declare `__gorget_clone_site_hit` as an external fn in the emitted LLVM IR, (c) survive that path's `.replace("static ", "")` strip — which would give the counter table + hit fn EXTERNAL linkage and let a shared-lib + exe build bind both to ONE wrongly-sized table (silent misattribution; the C path avoids this by keeping the hit fn `static`). Compile-time modes (`sites`/`verbose`/`sites-tsv=PATH`) already work under LLVM. Bounded; own scout.

(4) cc is LINEAR in C size (no super-linearity); emitted-C expansion ratio grew only 17.3×→19.3× (+22%/fn from drop-elab/lazy-iter/FaultCheck/clone-insertion = real work, NOT a mono explosion). **ACTIONABLE LEVERS (owner-decision; none are regressions to fix, they're speedups):** (a) **run the heavy C self-host tests under LLVM / drop from the default C sweep → ~250–300s saving, zero compiler work** (the self-host serial cluster = 632s/74% of the 859s; LLVM already avoids the 5× 27MB-cc, hence LLVM 540–587s vs C 859s) — tradeoff: C-backend bootstrap leaves the default C sweep; (b) build bootstrap stages with `cc -O1` not `-O0` (~40–65s; probe first); (c) continue clone-elimination (10.3M clones/517MB still). **Do NOT pursue:** overflow gate (0.17%), a mono-explosion fix (none exists), TU-split for per-byte cc (linear), lowering `MAX_GEN` (already breaks at convergence).
#### 🆕 THE WASTED-CLONE DETECTOR (owner-proposed 2026-07-19; the 4th instrument — MED, own scout; lands BEFORE/WITH the SH bare-arg CoW fix as its meter)
- **Dynamic dead-clone detection: count clones where NEITHER source nor copy is written afterward — the clone was provably unnecessary.** The dual of the dead-write warning (write-to-copy-nobody-reads ⟷ copy-nobody-writes). Mechanism (opt-in `--clones=waste` or extend `stats`; zero-cost off): register (site-id, src-buf, new-buf) per instrumented clone + per-buffer dirty bit; every MUTATING runtime entry point (push/set/insert/string-mut — the funnel CoW already forces mutations through) dirties its buffer; at free/exit, an un-dirtied pair = a WASTED clone for that site. Report `[waste-stats]` + per-site TSV → new columns in `clone_attribution.sh`: (site, reason, hits, wasted, waste%). **Per-site waste% = the sharpest charter meter** (100%-waste site = proven borrow candidate; grades even justified categories — a 99%-wasted consuming-position clone = a liveness gap). v0 CAVEAT: direct field stores bypass runtime entry points → v0 under-detects mutations, so waste verdicts are LEADS needing verification, not auto-fix truths; scout decides funnel-only (cheap) vs backend mark-dirty-on-store in instrumented mode (precise). Scalars/trivials OUT of v1. Serves: the SH bare-arg gap burn-down (ranked per-lane waste list), the per-reason budgets (a quality dimension), and the eventual static-warn subset (intraprocedurally-provable wasted clones → the warning family, per the wasteful-warns/unsound-rejects ruling).

- **🆕🧹 [Core #6 guard — CI-triage 2026-07-23] The ggdef corpus gates drift RED whenever a round adds an out-of-subset `cow_*` fixture, and the STOP-and-report is FAIL-FAST so each fix reveals the next.** `gate_fixtures` auto-discovers every `cow_*`/`deadwrite_*` fixture (by design — a new fixture must be adjudicated or explicitly excluded), but the EXCLUDE lists are hand-maintained **in two files** (`spec/ggdef/tests/corpus_b.rs`, `corpus_b1.rs`) — a parallel-list smell (Layering rule 3). This has now bitten at least twice: corpus_b1's own comment records the CoW-2G rows landing without a refresh, and the 2026-07-21 round landed FOUR (`cow_getref_dict_writethrough` + the three `cow_trait_*`) leaving CI red 2 days. Wanted: (a) report ALL out-of-subset fixtures in one run instead of panicking on the first (collect + assert once, like the corpus tables do for MISMATCH) so a refresh is one pass, not N; (b) one shared exclusion source of truth keyed by fixture with its gate-citation, consumed by both gates; (c) ideally derive the exclusion from a frontmatter/`known_gaps` marker on the fixture itself so adding the fixture and declaring its subset status is ONE edit at the source.
- **🆕⚖ [LOW — adjudication coverage] Promote the `retborrow_*` boundary fixtures into the ggdef adjudication corpus.** They are CoW-boundary fixtures but are deliberately named `retborrow_*` rather than `cow_*` so they do not drift the two auto-discovering ggdef gates (each hard-asserts a fixture count). Promoting them means renaming to the `cow_` family AND refreshing both counts + `EXCLUDE` lists with a cited comment, per fixture, after checking each is in ggdef's subset. Worth doing once the shared-exclusion cleanup above lands — doing it before just doubles the hand-maintained parallel lists.

- **🆕🐛 [macOS dev-box false RED] `security_safe_no_leak` mis-reports on any platform without LeakSanitizer.** The 5 `cow_bareassign_*_no_leak` tests fail on macOS with `AddressSanitizer: detect_leaks is not supported on this platform` — the harness's `has_leak_report` scrape (`tests/security.rs:271-275`) matches the substring `"AddressSanitizer"`, which that very *unsupported* notice contains, so the guard fires on its own inapplicability. Real leaks are still gated on Linux/CI. Fix: detect the unsupported notice and SKIP (loudly) rather than fail, or gate the no-leak assertions on `cfg!(target_os = "linux")` the way `spec_conformance`'s `floor_active` already does — a permanently-red local suite trains contributors to ignore it.

The fault-catch EBinaryOp path gates fault-category on the typed `binop_code` (`==OP_ADD`→overflow check, `==OP_DIV`→div-split), so a FUTURE fault-catch operator token NOT explicitly wired into `map_binop` would silently map to `OP_ADD` AND get routed into the overflow-FaultCheck path. **Current Inc2 is SAFE** — the div-overflow discriminator `OP_DIV_OVERFLOW` is a hardcoded constant (`lower_expr.gg:918`), NOT routed through `map_binop`; no new operator token was added. ⚠ NOW LOAD-BEARING FOR D26/Batch-C1 (2026-07-11): the fallible-operator tokens (`+! -! *! /! %! <<! >>! **!` + `**`) are exactly the future-operator class this entry guards — the C1 executor MUST wire each into `map_binop` explicitly + land the `tests/lints.rs` anti-`OP_ADD`-default ratchet as part of C1 (mirror `no_bare_collection_accumulator_outside_helper`).

The zeroing is half-right (a closure may run after/outside the block); conservative-correct fix direction: keep the zeroing for the body walk but gate ESCAPING mut-captures of outer vars at closure-definition-inside-arena, or track "closure defined under arena_depth>0" and apply the producer at its call sites inside the block. NOT covered by the landed core (which gates statement/expr positions only). Probe `p18_closure_escape.gg` in `/tmp/recover_r33_arenaescape_probes.tar.gz`.
  - **🐛 NEW (same scout) N1 sub-hole (b): nested-arena depth-blindness (LOGICAL, ASan-INVISIBLE).** `arena_scoped_vars` is a flat set — no depth tag — so inner-arena→outer-arena-var escapes (`vo = !vi` with `vo` under the OUTER `with Arena`, `vi` under the INNER) skip every gate (`target ∈ arena_scoped_vars`). Runtime happens to read valid bytes because the inner arena's storage is drawn FROM the outer arena (nesting rule) and bump free is a no-op — logically dangling, physically intact until the OUTER destroy, invisible to ASan without arena poisoning (probes `p17`/`p17b`: check-clean, run-CLEAN). Fix (follow-up to the landed N1 core): tag `arena_scoped_vars` values with their `arena_depth` and reject on depth mismatch (inner→outer), or poison arena regions on destroy under `--sanitize`.

- **🛡 STRUCTURAL GUARD — producer-side leak validator (design grounded; NEEDS the ≥3-pass plan-review before execute).** Converts the leak-freedom campaign from per-site hand-fixes to a build-breaking invariant. **Invariant:** every value produced as `Owned`/`FreshOwned` of a droppable type must, before fn exit, be registered for drop (`Drop`/`DropIfAlive`) OR moved out (`MoveZero`). Existing guards police CONSUME (Tier 2a `validate_consume_sites`, 2c `validate_drop_pre_rebind`) + TYPE (`validate_drop_completeness`); NONE policies PRODUCTION (scout-confirmed gap). **SELF-HOST FIRST:** add `Vector[String] validate_owned_temps_registered_or_moved(GirModule &gmod)` to `tests/fixtures/self_host_lowerer/validate.gg` (wired into `run_validators`/`validate_gir_after` `:288`/`:361`), keying on `is_droppable_type` (`lower.gg:1161`), scanning `GIDrop`/`GIDropIfAlive`/`GIMoveZero`, gated `GG_VALIDATE_OWNED_TEMP_DROP`. **Rust sibling** in `src/ir/validate.rs` reusing `collect_drop_registered_locals` (`:1999`) + a `MoveZero` scan; owned-ness on `Local.ownership ∈ {Owned,FreshOwned}` (`src/ir/mod.rs:447/667`), droppability on `needs_drop`/`is_resource_type`. Lifecycle (devbook/25): env-gate→triage→burn to 0→fatal. **Risks:** (1) lights up the `!`-param-no-drop gap (`drop_loop_reinit`) — blocks 'fatal' until closed/carved; (2) registered-but-conditionally-moved must PASS; (3) Rust-only str-view-escape (`drops.rs:226-228`) + CoW-string-alias (`assigns.rs:660-687/706/730`) carve-outs (self-host emits `GIMoveZero` on every move so unaffected); (4) static-literal `*_materialize` triage. ## Concurrency

### High

- **🆕 ROUND-33 shared-override follow-ups (filed at inc-1 landing per output-review):** (a) self-host ESpawn raw-passthrough is WHOLE-CALL not per-arg (mixed auto+override spawn would mis-pass the auto carrier raw); (b) `lower_shared_spawn` doesn't gate on kind (`&x` of an override-kind var → mutex-shaped token wrapper → loud cc-fail, not silent); (c) **Rust-side Core #8 candidate: Rust gg rwlock compound-assign takes the WRITE LOCK BEFORE lowering the RHS (`assigns.rs:1085-1105`) → `x += f(x)` SELF-DEADLOCKS in Rust; the self-host lowers RHS first and is correct — fix Rust.** (a)/(b) subsumed by the round-16 real-spawn keystone but distinct sites.

- **🆕 ROUND-39 T2 spawn/blocking follow-ups (filed at landing per brief; the +5 fixtures now MATCH, these are the residual semantic gaps — each output-invisible on the current corpus):**
  - **(1) Synchronous inline-closure-spawn model.** The self-host lowers `spawn ((): body)()` (inline closure literal) SYNCHRONOUSLY — the closure body runs on the CURRENT thread at the spawn site and the paired `.await()` is a no-op (the `case EClosure` arm in `lower_expr.gg` ESpawn→ECall now dispatches `__callable_<arity>` inline, same model as the already-passing `spawn_closure_copy`/`spawn_closure_void` closure-VAR path). Rust threads it via `build_spawn_wrapper` (a real spawned thread). CORRECT-OUTPUT ONLY for concurrency-agnostic fixtures — the 3 that landed (`spawn_closure_inline`/`spawn_unchecked`/`spawn_unchecked_bypasses_check`) all await immediately after spawn, so output is deterministic regardless of sync-vs-thread. A fixture whose output depends on real parallelism/interleaving would be right-by-luck. Fix = materialize the inline closure into a real spawn wrapper (mirror Rust `build_spawn_wrapper`); pairs with the Group-A pool gap below.
  - **(2) Anonymous awaited-String-temp leak (14B on `spawn_blocking_multi`) — PRE-EXISTING in the shared real-spawn/await path, NOT introduced by T2, NOT blocking-specific.** `print(t.await())` on a `Task[String]` leaks 14 bytes in 3 allocations (`str_alloc_copy` ← `gorget_file_read_all` ← `blocking_read`, via `__gorget_global_alloc_fn`): the awaited-String temp passed DIRECTLY to a call arg is never drop-registered. It is the anonymous-temp shape that leaks, NOT the await count — `spawn_blocking_basic` (`String result = t.await(); print(result)`) is ASan-CLEAN because the awaited result binds to a NAMED local that IS drop-registered (`gorget_string_free(&result)` emitted). **Re-verified 2026-07-04 by rebuilding the committed driver + ASan matrix: basic CLEAN, multi 14B → 4/5 clean.** The 14B reproduces IDENTICALLY in a regular (NON-blocking) `spawn areader(...)` awaited inline in `print` (`reg_inline` repro), and vanishes when that same regular spawn binds the result to a named local (`reg_named` clean) — so the leak lives in the shared await-result-temp lowering, exercised by both regular `spawn` and `spawn blocking`. Same CLASS as the already-filed trait-call-return-value temp leak (`print(s.speak())`, in this file's ROLLING queue item 6) — a call/await result temp consumed as an arg isn't drop-registered. Fix = drop-register the owned result temp at the consuming call-arg / auto-prop site (must be conditional to avoid a double-free when the result also binds to a VarDecl — deep drop-machinery, not a spawn-local patch; do NOT chase in a spawn track). ⚠ A pre-fix bare-passthrough driver mis-attributes this as a 21B `spawn_blocking_basic` "sync-inline" leak — that is the OLD behavior (direct `blocking_read` call, no spawn runtime); rebuild the driver from the committed `lower_expr.gg` before measuring.
  - **(3) Group-A blocking-pool routing divergence.** T2 routes `spawn blocking f()` through the SAME `lower_named_fn_spawn` wiring that regular `spawn` uses — main() emits REAL spawn/await (`__gorget_spawn_<fn>` → `GORGET_SCHEDULER_SUBMIT` on a pthread-backed `GorgetTask`, `__gorget_await_<fn>` → `GORGET_SCHEDULER_WAIT`; NOT sync-inline), but on the SAME fixed-size executor with no blocking-pool accounting. Rust routes it to an EXPANDABLE blocking pool (`blocking_fn_names`/`has_blocking_pool` → `BLOCKING_POOL_RUNTIME`). OUTPUT-INVISIBLE for the 5 fixtures (the emitted VALUE is identical — the awaited real return value — so the flip is reference-grade on OUTPUT), but a latent LIVENESS gap: fixed-executor saturation under many concurrent blocking tasks could deadlock where Rust's expandable pool would not. Fix = give the self-host a blocking-pool tag + expandable-pool runtime, or document the fixed-executor semantics as intentional.

- **ROUND-16 LANDED (Phase-1 `f8357b1f` + close: Phase-2 await-splice `0a8ee959`, Shared[T]-wrapper LIR prerequisite `1acac6ee`, `??`/`*x`-reject `7e341681`+`e27b9c25` — all in DONE; re-measure parity with the runtime_diff command at the top of this file). ⏭ REMAINING from the round-16 (A)/(B)/(C) increments:** **(B) 🔴 RUST-HALF Core #8 — the spawn-ctx refcounted-carrier RETAIN-WITHOUT-RELEASE leak class (output-review `aa226fd4` MEASURED, BROADER than Shared) — STILL PENDING, own Rust round:** Rust's spawn-ctx emit CLONES the carrier into the heap ctx (`emit_types.rs:472-474` `{gir}__clone`) but NEVER emits the balancing release in EITHER teardown path (`__spawn_drop` `:449-456`, `__gorget_await` `:494-505`) → leaks EVERY refcounted spawn carrier (measured: Shared `shared_stale_blocking` 176B, Channel `async_channel` 264+40B). PLUS the wrapper-routing bug `helpers.rs:250` (`Shared__Mutex__T__drop`→`gorget_shared_drop` frees only the 8B data buffer, not the inner mutex — route to `gorget_shared_mutex_drop` when `type_name.contains("__Mutex__")`; the runtime fn already exists). The self-host already does BOTH correctly (Phase-1 + the keystone). PRE-EXISTING (NOT introduced by round-16); per Core #8 NOT a benign-filed punt — full corpus + ASan re-sweep (touches Channel parity) + leak fixtures. **Pairs with the filed `register_param` direct-call UAF + `ae887786` Channel double-free = the Rust-side refcounted-carrier-balance campaign.** **(A) Phase-2 await-splice = LANDED** (`0a8ee959`; 2 follow-ups filed below — the await-detector if-expr coverage gap + single-shared-arg wrapper). **(C) explicit-`Shared[T]`-method gap: the LIR wrapper synthesis = LANDED** (`1acac6ee` — `gorget_shared_{get,at,set_at,slen}` now defined + a real `gorget_shared_new` size-truncation fix); the 8 fixtures still don't flip — REMAINING work is the **frontend blocker cluster** (4 `lower_expr.gg` bugs, bug #1 `Shared.clone()` double-free HIGH) filed in its own bullet below.

- **🆕 ROUND-16 PHASE-2 FOLLOW-UPS (filed 2026-06-30 by the Phase-2 await-splice output-review `aad66092`; the splice itself LANDED `0a8ee959` — see DONE; both are narrowings vs the Rust ref, NOT exercised by the 2 shipped fixtures, so not a shipped-behavior defect — but both are genuine pending gaps):**
  - **🐛 LATENT Core #8 soundness gap — the self-host body-await detector misses if-expr/block-expr/closure-expr suspensions.** `stmts_have_await`/`expr_has_await` (`lower_generics.gg:~1953`) counts `EAwait` + blocking-call names but omits the block-expr, if-expr, and closure-expr forms the Rust ref covers (`src/ir/lowering/context.rs:3722-3759`). An async callee whose ONLY suspension is buried in an if-expression VALUE (or block/closure expr) mis-routes to the SYNC `__shared_token_` path → **lock held across the await** (the exact deadlock the design prevents). Not reachable by `shared_with_spawned_refresh`/`shared_nested_spawn` (statement-level awaits), so latent — but a real Core #8 hole. Fix = extend the self-host detector to mirror `expr_has_await`'s expr-form coverage; add a fixture with an if-expr-value suspension in a spawned async callee.
  - **Single-shared-arg async wrapper.** `build_shared_async_wrapper` processes only `pend.shared_arg_indices.get(0)` (`lower_expr.gg:~1417`); the Rust ref loops over ALL shared args (`shared_async.rs:119,134`) and the self-host's own SYNC twin already loops. A multi-shared-arg async callee would silently process only the first. Mirror the loop. (Cosmetic adjacent: the now-read-dead `async_fns` local param threaded through `scan_spawn_wrappers`/`scan_expr_for_spawn` — routing moved to typed `gmod.async_fns`; remove the dead thread when touching this.)

Under LeakSanitizer BOTH binaries run clean (ctx stays reachable via the detached thread `pthread_create`+`pthread_detach`), so it's not LSan-detectable, and the self-host is at BEHAVIORAL PARITY with Rust (`spawn_join_on_drop` MATCHes).     - **SEMANTICS ARE SETTLED — a lock IS a Resource (do NOT re-open as an "owner decision"; my earlier framing was over-cautious):** `language-design.md:319` (Resource = "owns a heap allocation, file handle, **or lock** — cannot be implicitly copied"); `devbook/18-runtime-abi.md:84-99` (Mutex is an opaque handle COVERING a runtime-owned allocation — "the runtime owns both halves"); `book/14-concurrency.md:135` + `book/16-smart-pointers.md:75` (the ARC owns the Mutex, "dropped when the last owner goes away"); `language-reference.md:3589` (guards drop to release the lock). The runtime ships a `gorget_mutex_new`/`gorget_mutex_free` PAIR and never calls free. The lone dissenter is the type metadata (`builtins.rs:587-588` `MUTEX = CopySemantics::Trivial, drop_fn: None`; self-host mirrors via `GtPtr(Mutex__int64_t)` + `is_droppable_type(GtPtr)=false` `lower_drops.gg:160-163`). The double-free worry is INVERTED: Resource = no-implicit-copy → single owner → single drop, so the Resource classification PREVENTS the double-free.
    - **(a) BOTH compilers leak, across BOTH forms (RUN-measured):** bare `Mutex[int]` (`mutex_basic`) leaks 136B; the dominant **`Shared[Mutex[T]]`** form (`shared_float`, via the `shared`-keyword CFA promotion) leaks **176B / 4 allocations in Rust gg** (trace `Shared__Mutex__double__new → main`). So this is not a bare-Mutex corner — the ARC-wrapped canonical form leaks too.
    - **(b) The fix is the ARC-CONTENT-DEEP-DROP branch, NOT a Mutex flag-flip:** `shared_float`'s 4 leaked allocations (ARC control block + Mutex + boxed value + …) show `Shared`'s drop-at-refcount-0 does NOT dispatch its inner type's `drop_fn` today — a broad `Shared[anything-droppable]` hole. The fix must teach the `Shared` content-drop path to deep-drop its inner (which then frees Mutex AND any droppable inner uniformly), in BOTH compilers, alongside flipping `MUTEX`→`Resource`+`gorget_mutex_free` (and the self-host `GtPtr`→droppable + `drop_fn_for_type` Mutex/RWLock arms + `GIDropIfAlive` ptr-slot load). Confirm there's no path where a bare Mutex handle is a NON-owning view (`devbook/18:98` notes some opaque handles ARE non-owning — but `gorget_mutex_new` always-allocates says Mutex is owning; RUN-confirm).

- **🆕 CI/TOOLING FOLLOW-UPS (filed 2026-06-30, from the `test_result_advanced` CI-red fix — see DONE):**
  - **🔧 macOS dev machine CANNOT run the self-host runtime net / bootstrap locally without a `cc` shim — this is WHY the `Result.map` drop-corruption shipped (4 days red on CI, invisible locally).** The self-host's emitted C contains constructs that Linux/gcc accepts as WARNINGS but Apple clang (the dev toolchain) rejects as ERRORS, so `self_host_runtime` + `self_host_bootstrap_fixed_point` CC-FAIL on ~925/~36/8 fixtures locally for reasons UNRELATED to any given change → the dev never sees real runtime regressions; CI (Linux/gcc) is the only place these fixtures execute. **Workaround proven this round:** a PATH-shimmed `cc` wrapping `/usr/bin/cc -Wno-int-conversion -Wno-implicit-function-declaration -Wno-incompatible-pointer-types -Wno-return-mismatch -Wno-return-type` makes the net regress down to 8 genuinely-platform-specific fixtures (the `send`/`clone_inplace`/`RxNode` macOS-header/fwd-decl clashes + `meta_platform_guard` OS-detection). **Fix options (own decision):** (a) make the test harness append these `-Wno-*` flags to its `cc` invocations on macOS (`tests/integration.rs` `self_host_emit_cc_run` :18426 + `self_host_bootstrap_fixed_point` :18633) so local validation is faithful; OR (b) document the shim in CLAUDE.md Build&Test as the macOS prerequisite for self-host gates. Without one of these, every self-host codegen change is un-validatable on the dev box and ships blind to CI.
  - **🐛 CLEAN UP the gcc-tolerated-but-clang-rejected self-host EMIT classes (latent UB, benign on the gcc/x86_64 production target but real codegen sloppiness).** Surfaced as the ~36 CC-FAIL classes when the net runs under strict clang: (i) **int↔pointer untyped-local stores** — the self-host emits `void* local = (int32_t)0` for some unresolved-type locals (dead stores in `test_result_advanced`, but the class is real: a local whose type the lowerer couldn't resolve defaults to `void*` + an int-0 init); (ii) **non-void functions that fall off the end without a `return`** (`-Wreturn-mismatch`: `Column__col_len`, `IoError__display`, `MapIter__…__next`, `Color__eq`, etc. — the self-host emits a non-void signature but no terminal return on some path); (iii) **conflicting forward-decl vs definition types** (`X__clone_inplace`, `RxNode`) and the **`send` libc-symbol collision** (the self-host emits a function named `send`, clashing with macOS `<sys/socket.h>`; rename-mangle runtime-colliding symbols). All round-trip harmlessly on 64-bit gcc but are principled bugs; (i) is also the reason the `map` crash was a SEGFAULT rather than a clean error on x86_64. Own scout per class; lower priority than parity work but they erode the "reference-grade emit" bar.

Sub-requirements (the self-host must own the WHOLE pipeline — today `driver.gg` is source→C, the harness does cc): (1) cc/link ownership in the self-host driver; (2) full CLI surface (`build`/`run`/`check`, `-o`, flags); (3) runtime + `lib/std` bundling (mirror the Rust gg resource-schema `include_str!`); (4) relocate self-host source out of `tests/fixtures/`; (5) the `GG_IMPL` build orchestrator (Makefile/`build.sh`/`gg bootstrap`). Bootstrap stays: Rust gg (or a stage0 blob) is the build-time SEED; `GG_IMPL` picks the INSTALLED binary. **Near-term value = parity ACCELERATOR** (a standalone `gg-selfhost` lets us dogfood REAL programs, surfacing gaps the fixture corpus misses). Runs PARALLEL to parity tracks (disjoint files). Foundational milestone = `GG_IMPL=selfhost` build → a `gg-selfhost build hello.gg -o hello && ./hello` that works standalone.

REMAINING:** **sub-req 2** (CLI surface) PARTIAL — `check`/`--help`/`--backend`/`--emit-*`/`--version`/`-V` + unknown-command diagnostic + the pipeline test landed (G1+G2 landed `1d6d33a8`, 2026-06-24, scout-validated 4-review); build/run/-o were already there. REMAINING (pure plumbing, low value): `lex`/`parse` as real commands need format-matched output (NOT plumbing — defer); broader error coverage gated on the typechecker-permissiveness gap (separate, filed). G3 (stricter unknown-flag/`-o`-no-value validation) is REFUTED — Rust gg is itself permissive there, so tightening would be a divergence. **sub-req 3** (runtime + `lib/std` bundling = the relocatable-single-binary ENDGAME BLOCKER) DONE through Inc-3 (a `gg-selfhost` for import-free + `lib/std` programs is ALREADY fully relocatable, no env); REMAINING: Inc-3b (`lib/xtd`, 876KB — most pull SQLite/SDL/GL externs) + Inc-4 (SQLite, conditional) — both later/optional. **sub-req 4 (relocate `driver.gg` out of `tests/fixtures/`) — DEFERRED, needs its OWN scout (scoping-scout 2026-06-24 measured HIGH/broad blast radius, NOT a safe single round, NOT parallel-safe):** 27+7 harness refs to `self_host_lowerer` paths, ~15 `tests/lints.rs` paths that would SILENTLY NEUTER (read-empty → 0 violations → false green, Core-#6 rot trap), 90 depth-sensitive `embed_file("../../../…")` paths in `driver.gg`, and 15 symlinks to `self_host_typechecker/` (its OWN comparison fixture — can't move with it). Lower-risk path: a shipped dir at the SAME repo-depth + path-indirection + re-pointed symlinks + a lints.rs silent-guard audit — pick the depth-preserving target dir up front. Do as its own scout→brief→reviews→executor when funded. **sub-req 5** (orchestrator) MOSTLY done (`scripts/gg_impl.sh` both modes); after Inc-2+3 it can drop the `GG_RUNTIME_DIR`/`GG_LIB_DIR` exports for a truly relocatable install — wire that up. Near-term value = the parity ACCELERATOR (found the reference-grade ill-typed-program-acceptance defect, filed below; + the GG_IMPL `run`-mode bare-name exec bug in High Priority).
  - **🐛 accelerator find #2 (harness exit-code coverage) — REFRAMED (the "EXIT-CODE-BLIND" claim was OVER-STATED; output-review `a5b48428` corrected it).** `self_host_runtime_diff` does NOT silently MATCH a wrong exit code: `self_host_emit_cc_run` (`tests/integration.rs:16449`) returns `Crashed{exit_code}` on any non-zero self-host exit, and the oracle path excludes oracle-non-zero fixtures — so a wrong non-zero exit surfaces as CRASH, not a false MATCH. The void-main bug was invisible only because NO corpus fixture used the tail-`print` `void main` form (all 1016 mains are block-body). A `GG_RUNTIME_DIFF_EXIT=1` exact-exit-vs-oracle guard would be near-vacuous (expected-zero); low-pri. The `print_tail_exit_*` regression fixtures (landed) are the real guard.
- **🔧 `--release` (-O2) silently IGNORED on 3 build sub-paths** (the main `build`/`run` paths WORK — see DONE.md). `release` isn't consumed at: (a) the `--shared` library build (`src/main.rs:838-887`, own cc invocation early-returning before the `:1103` `-O2` site → `gg build --shared --release` silently emits a `-O0` lib); (b) the freestanding/UEFI cc path (same early-return); (c) the `gg script.gg` shorthand (`src/main.rs:~2476` never threads `release` into `LoweringOptions`). Same gap-class — wire `release` into those 3 sites' opt-level (or scope the `appendix-cli.md` wording). Nothing breaks (default intact); completeness gap. Low-pri.

- **🐛 GG_IMPL self-host `exec` is shell-based (`system()` → `sh -c`) — word-splits on spaces/shell-metacharacters in paths** (filed by the run-mode output-review `afd371e2`, 2026-06-22; PRE-EXISTING, NOT introduced by the run-mode fix `d2e64d22`). The self-host driver's `exec` (`std.process`, runtime `gorget_exec` → `process_runtime.c:5-6` `system(cmd)`) is used for BOTH the run path (`driver.gg:704`, now `path_absolute`-resolved) AND the `cc_cmd` (`driver.gg:695-696`) — so a build/run path or `-o` target containing a space or shell metacharacter word-splits or injects. Rust gg avoids this entirely by exec'ing the built binary shell-free via `Command::new(&exe_path).status()` (`src/main.rs:2489-2497`) and building the cc argv as a vector. Reference-grade fix = give the self-host a shell-free exec (argv vector, no `sh -c`) for both sites. Lower-pri (no corpus path has spaces today); doesn't trip Core-#8 (pre-existing, unchanged). Own small scout→fix. (Cosmetic sibling: `tests/integration.rs:~16292` `self_host_cli_pipeline` step-6 comment says "NOT where the .gg lives" but the cwd IS where it lives — the bite is the bare `-o` name; fix the comment on contact.)
- **LSP server.** `language-design.md` lists it but nothing ships. The gorget-js agent flagged it as the single biggest DX gap (~50% of loops were "what's that field/method spelled?"). Biggest payoff, biggest investment — don't start until the smaller papercuts are done; multi-week. The semantic analyzer already builds a complete typed scope tree (`ScopeTable` + `function_info` + `TypeRegistry`); the work is the LSP-protocol layer + file-watching + the hover design pass.

- **[LOW — deferred tidy, discovered during D21 `gg sim` deletion 2026-07-24] `regex = "1"` in `Cargo.toml:26`'s `[dependencies]` is now used only by `tests/lints.rs` (a test target)** — move it to `[dev-dependencies]`. Self-contained, optional, out of scope for the D21 round itself.

- **[LOW — honest coverage note, from D21 `gg sim` deletion 2026-07-24] No exact live successor for sim's type-agnostic `UninitializedRead` slot-liveness check** (was `src/sim/dispatch.rs:815+`, now deleted). `--sanitize` wires ASan+UBSan (`-fsanitize=address,undefined`) but NOT MemorySanitizer, and UBSan only validates `bool`/`enum` load-validity, not a generic uninitialized read of an arbitrary-typed local. Plausibly unreachable from valid Gorget source (type-first decls mandate initializers, so an uninit read would require a lowering bug), and sim only ever checked its OWN GIR execution, never the real backend — so no user-facing guarantee was lost by the deletion. If a future lowering bug ever produces an uninit read, the successor is wiring MSan into `--sanitize` or a static check, NOT resurrecting the interpreter.

#### Toolchain exit-code scheme follow-ups (filed 2026-07-15 — production polish, surfaced by the exit-code research backing the ggdef elab∘eval verdict-triple; scheme RATIFIED Option A in decisions.md: 0 success / 1 static-rejection / 2 usage / 101 trap+ICE / 103 ggdef-only fuel)
- **[LOW — production `gg`, consensus polish] Usage/CLI errors collapse into exit 1 instead of 2.** Per the ratified scheme, genuine usage errors (bad flags, missing file, misinvocation) should exit **2** (GNU/argparse consensus), distinct from a *source* static-rejection (exit 1). Production `gg` currently exits 1 at ~50 `process::exit(1)` sites in `src/main.rs`, conflating "you invoked me wrong" with "your source is wrong." Audit those sites, split genuine usage errors to 2, leave parse/semantic/build failures at 1. Not conformance-compared (ggdef's own usage=2 already correct); reference-grade legibility polish. Pairs with the ggdef `EXIT_USAGE=2` that already holds.
- **[LOW — production `gg`, Core-#8 flavor] Internal runtime panics exit 1, colliding with the compile-error code.** `gorget_panic_at` (`src/backend/c/runtime/panic_normal.c:5`) handles *internal* runtime failures (OOM, closed-channel send, file-open error) and exits **1** — numerically colliding with the static-rejection/compile-error code, so a shell script can't distinguish "your program failed to compile" from "your program ran and hit an internal panic." Per the ratified taxonomy these are *runtime* failures and should either be traps (exit 101, via `gorget_trap_at`) or get their own distinct code. Decide which (are these D11 catchable traps or a separate ICE-adjacent class?), then reroute + a both-backend test. Distinct from the user-facing `gorget_trap_at`=101 path which is already correct.

- **[LOW] 🐞 DWARF line-table debug info (gdb/lldb stepping).** `Backend.debug_info: bool` exists but is hardwired `false` (`src/backend/c_lir/mod.rs:~3438`). The C backend could get source-line stepping nearly free via `#line` directives mapping emitted C back to `.gg` source; LLVM needs `!dbg` metadata. Distinct from (and cheaper than) the existing Low entry on `caller_location()`/multi-frame backtraces. Unblocks "can I actually debug a Gorget program" — table stakes before 1.0, after LSP.

### Medium

#### 🆕 BOOK-FOLLOW AUDIT #1 FINDINGS (filed 2026-07-18; the first newcomer-simulation audit, ch1-3 — full report was /tmp/bookaudit_report.md, re-runnable; ch1 10/10, ch2 6/10, ch3 7/10)
- **[HIGH — language semantics, ledger-grade] Narrow-type overflow is unenforced: `uint8 255 + 1` → 256 (no panic), `255 +% 1` → 256 (no wrap).** The book says "integer overflow always panics"; only 64-bit `int` does. Narrow types appear to be int64 in disguise — neither trapping nor wrapping at their declared width. Needs a D-decision (what IS uint8 arithmetic?) + conformance fixtures once ruled; check D26 (`+!`/ArithError) for the intended model first.
- **[MED — emit] f-string mixing `int` + `float` leaks C `%lld`-vs-`int64_t` format warnings to the user's terminal** (aarch64: int64_t is `long`, emitted spec is `%lld`). Fix the emitted format (PRId64-style), not a `-w` blanket — hits the book's own first multi-type example.
- **[LOW — UX] `gg build` leaves the emitted `.c` in the cwd** — route to a temp/build dir unless `--emit-c` is asked.
- **[LOW — book, one-liners batched for the next docs touch] ch2 `float64 precise = 3.14` example doesn't compile (type table says `float`); ch3 enum example omits the value construction (`Direction direction = North`); ch3 match section uses enum/Option/Result with no forward reference to ch6/ch9; ch3 while-else/`loop:` snippets call undefined helpers so they can't run as printed.**
